Synthetic snapshot from hooks; carriers are title suffix, pane fourth line, exposer hook trace until a native persistent status-line API exists.
camsessid Neutral short id carried in title/pane identity. Often supplied by CLAUDE_SHORT; name is compatibility-only. Often supplied by CODEX_SHORT from HOOK_SESSION_ID.
camcarrier Surface selected to carry a projection. Native statusLine preferred; title/pane/exposer fallback. Title suffix and pane fourth line preferred; exposer only for event traces.
camlabel Explicit human/operator text carried by title or pane. label, title suffix, or pane fourth line passed through hooks or ccam. Same params passed through Codex hooks or ccam.
camfocus Active task/current work focus. Task substrate, active-work awakening summary, or statusLine adapter. Task substrate, Codex hook snapshot, or explicit control param.
camstate Operator-visible runtime state. Hook event/statusLine state. Hook event, running shell/tool state, footer evidence.
camqueue Compact task counts. Task substrate glance. Task substrate glance.
camtokens / camcontext Budget and context facts. StatusLine payload or native footer evidence when available. Codex footer/status evidence when readable, or explicit hook/control param.
camhooktrace Event-worthy hook output visible in the exposer. Claude hook output/statusMessage lines. Codex hook output/statusMessage lines.
camtaskstrip Native progress/task furniture visible in a clengine UI. Claude task list and shell/progress strip. Codex may expose different task/progress hints; not guaranteed equivalent.
camstatus Native runtime status text not owned by Solarspace. Claude working time, running shell count, tokens, permission/status footer. Codex working time, token budget, shell count, permission/status footer.

Adapter Rule

The adapter maps neutral state into native surfaces:

source domes -> camstatline.snapshot.v1 -> clengine adapter -> visible carrier

Claude's preferred carrier is a native status line command when present. Codex's preferred carriers are camtitle micro suffix and campane compact line, with camexposer reserved for event-worthy traces.

Reconciliation Rule

Two cams reconcile when their neutral components are equivalent, not when their pixels are equivalent. A Claude cam and a Codex cam may render different native footer, task, permission, and model furniture while still satisfying the same Solarspace component contract.

For comparison, inspect in this order:

  1. camaddress: same ment, clengine, tty, session short, or declared fallback.
  2. camtitle: identity grammar and optional micro carrier.
  3. campane: identity badge and optional compact carrier.
  4. camexposer: transcript/hook evidence.
  5. camcomposer: safe input state.
  6. camcomposerchrome, camtaskstrip, camstatus: native evidence only.

Related

  • [[camera]] -- the occupied presence space.
  • [[clengine]] -- the agent loop whose native UI must be adapted.
  • [[cam-composer]] -- the canonized input surface.
  • [[reconciliation]] -- cross-surface equivalence without pixel identity.

Concept: campaign

2026-06-29 canon rename: campaign is now the first-class bounded scheduled work form, not a species hidden under recurrences. The typed record lives at work.campaigns.campaign.v1 under sol_work/sol_campaigns/, with CAM###-<slug>-<yyyymmdd>/campaign.yaml instance records. The old watch species is not part of campaigns; unbounded observation belongs under work/monitors.

A campaign is a bounded recurring series: an obligation that fires on a cadence (like a rhythm) but ends after a declared bound is met (unlike a rhythm, which recurs indefinitely). It is rhythm's bounded sibling.

A campaign may still keep a project as its auditable program-of-record, but its cadence+bound+ledger state is a typed campaign record, not a loose composition:

  • Program-of-record — a project (sol_work/sol_projects/records/J###-...): the auditable definition. Holds the membership roster, the per-unit checklist, and the goal.yaml until-condition that encodes the bound (count(done) == N). The project — not the scheduler — owns the count and the termination.
  • Cadence — a cron (sol_work/sol_crons): the recurring trigger (calendar + alarm). A pure trigger; it fires the unit, it does not decide when the campaign is done.
  • Outputs — post (or other unit) records, one per fire, each linked back to the program-of-record. The done-count is count(linked units, status=done).

The identity triple: cadence + bound + ledger

A campaign is defined by exactly three things; missing any one, it is not a campaign:

  1. Cadence — when it fires (the cron schedule).
  2. Bound — what ends it (the count == N until-condition in the project goal).
  3. Ledger — the fail-closed record of progress (which units done, who is next, never-double). The bound is enforced by the ledger, never by memory or a side-tracker: a unit not recorded done in the ledger is not done — loudly, never assumed (C153 fail-closed; C026 single shared store, not memory).

Invariants

  • Membership is declared at definition (D132). The roster is enumerated from canon once at def (e.g. the registry: collective.list.agents --role_class=...), then snapshotted into the program-of-record as a declared fact — frozen by the rename-proof key (agent_code), with display fields (name, color) resolved live at unit-time so no output shows a stale name. A member appearing mid-campaign is a new campaign, not a silent extension: a transition's membership is a declared fact, not an observed mood.
  • Schedule and execution are separable. The cadence fires; what the fire does is the executor's, and the unit may be gated (e.g. a real human interview + sign-off per C005, never auto-emitted). "Schedule + bound the obligation" (the campaign) is distinct from "execute the work" (the agent).
  • Termination is self-closing. When the ledger shows count(done) == N, the program-of-record closes and removes its own cron. The bound is reached in the auditable record and the trigger is retired by it — never left firing into an already-satisfied bound.
  • Roles are partitioned authorities over one record. Distinct owners — roster source, definition, trigger, sequence/state — each authoritative over its own subset, all bound to one shared program-of-record. This is plain single-source-of-truth. (Note: it is not an instance of C026's inbox-aggregation rule, which explicitly carves out partitioned-authority flows; C026 binds only the narrower claim that program state lives in one shared store, not memory.)

Instantiation hazard

Creating the program-of-record project must write at the correct id by direct write, not through a symlinked allocator (the J077 symlink-resolution hazard), or the campaign's home resolves to the wrong mentspace/id.

Relation to neighbors

  • monitor/watch — unbounded observation is not a campaign subtype; it belongs under monitors, which own ongoing watch/proof contracts.
  • project (one-shot bounded effort) — a campaign's program-of-record is a project; the campaign adds cadence + ledger on top.
  • commission (ONT078) — relational delegation with explicit closure; a campaign may be commissioned, but the campaign is the bounded-series form while the commission is the delegator↔delegate relationship.

Evidence / first instance: the Meet-the-nous 12-day interview-publish program (clawent) — one sister per day, bounded at 12, ledger-gated, roster canon-derived from the sister registry and snapshotted at def by agent_code.

Concept: campaigns

Campaigns are the work-substrate collection under sol_work/sol_campaigns/. The singular [[campaign]] term names the bounded scheduled-work concept; this plural term names the live folder, route, and identifier namespace used for campaign records.

Canonical instance shape:

  • folder: sol_work/sol_campaigns/records/CAM###-<slug>-<yyyymmdd>/
  • file: campaign.yaml
  • schema: work.campaigns.campaign.v1
  • route: work/campaigns/campaigns.*

The former sol_work/sol_recurrences/ namespace is superseded. Watch/unbounded records from that namespace migrate to monitors rather than campaigns.

Evidence

  • Producer: work@noument/ontology
  • Source scenario: do.ontology

Concept: canon

The canon is the collective's shared logic and data — what every ment holds in common rather than authors alone. It is disseminated through canonization: the custodian maintains the canonical copy and replicates it to all ments (D078; the update protocol is D120). The canon notably comprises the common logic and data, and the canonical self and work logic each ment mirrors.

Canon is the umbrella term; its facets are typed separately: the registry that names canonical artifacts (ONT087), the reference form (ONT088), the content authority (ONT089), its materialization (ONT090) and projection (ONT091), and the canonizing processes that move it (ONT106 fast, ONT107 deep). Canonenaction (ONT116) names the wider source-to-runtime adoption process that makes a canon change live for the collective. A ment's vendored canon mirror is received runtime state — never her own authored canon.

Computationally, canon is not a folder name, a live ls, a mirror, a byte match, or a hardcoded accepted-list. It has three distinct layers:

  1. Declared source-of-authority relation. A typed coordinate is declared canon by the canonical registry, content authority, and canon subdimension matrix: ONT087 names the registered form, ONT089 names authoritative content, and ONT118 names facet authority.
  2. Enaction process. A canon change becomes live only through ONT116 canonenaction: source update, materialization, projection, reception or adoption evidence, and closure.
  3. Conformance audit. A materialized path or runtime surface can be audited for conformance to the declared relation. That downstream audit is ONT122; it is not the definition of canon itself.

The source-authority predicate is coordinate-level:

is_declared_canon(coordinate) =
  registry_or_matrix_declares(coordinate)
  and authority(coordinate) == canon
  and source_ref(coordinate) exists
  and proof_class(coordinate) is named

The path predicate is only a conformance audit:

conforms_to_declared_canon?(ment, path, facet?) =
  resolve(path)
  and gate_admits(path)
  and authority(coordinate) == canon
  and source_ref(coordinate) exists
  and proof(path, source_ref, proof_class) passes

The liveness predicate is process-level:

is_live_canon(change_or_scope) =
  canonenaction_stages_closed(change_or_scope)
  and reception_or_adoption_evidence_exists

The facet coordinate and authority are governed by the ONT118 canon subdimension matrix. In particular, 0.0 is root.struct: the root manifest gate admitted by the canon matrix/package. The live root listing is a measurement compared against that gate; it is not the authority that defines the gate.

Audit surfaces are conformance machinery, not the source of canon meaning. U001 feeds structure and well-formedness. canon.audit owns facet-authority and materialization/projection conformance verdicts. A path-level audit should expose coordinate, gate_admitted, authority, source_ref, proof_class, declared_canon, materialization_conformant, enaction_status, and a conformance verdict from canon, own_valid, drift, residue, or projection_stale. A canon audit verdict means "conforms to a declared canon coordinate at this audit layer"; it does not by itself prove ONT116 live canon.

Concept: canon collapse

Canon collapse is the transition in which a mentspace's canon entangle (ONT246) — the shared basis/common canon held by symlink into noument's canonical directories — is replaced by real files and folders: the symlink is resolved into a materialized local copy.

After collapse the mentspace holds independent bytes rather than a live reference to the host. The canon is no longer shared-by-reference; it becomes a local instance that can thereafter diverge unless re-verified against the source.

Collapse is the mechanism for detaching or forking a mentspace from the live canon host — e.g. packaging a self-contained snapshot, or standing a mentspace up independently of noument. It is the inverse of remaining entangled: it trades single-source coherence for local independence, and so is performed deliberately, with provenance — never as incidental drift.

Concept: canon conformance audit predicate

The canon conformance audit predicate is the executable audit test for whether a materialized substrate path or runtime surface conforms to a declared canon source-of-authority relation.

This predicate is downstream of canon. It does not define canon by itself and it does not prove live canonenaction by byte-test alone. ONT115 defines canon as the declared shared source-of-authority relation plus the ONT116 enaction process that makes a canon change live. ONT122 audits whether a path or projection currently conforms to that declared relation.

The coordinate-level declaration predicate is:

is_declared_canon(coordinate) =
  registry_or_matrix_declares(coordinate)
  and authority(coordinate) == canon
  and source_ref(coordinate) exists
  and proof_class(coordinate) is named

The path-level conformance predicate is:

conforms_to_declared_canon?(ment, path, facet?) =
  resolve(path)
  and gate_admits(path)
  and authority(coordinate) == canon
  and source_ref(coordinate) exists
  and proof(path, source_ref, proof_class) passes

The live-canon question is separate:

is_live_canon(change_or_scope) =
  canonenaction_stages_closed(change_or_scope)
  and reception_or_adoption_evidence_exists

If any conformance step cannot be evaluated, the path-level result is not a canon conformance. It is residue, drift, own_valid, or projection_stale, depending on the failure mode. If conformance passes but enaction evidence is not checked, the path audit may report verdict=canon only in the narrow sense "conforms to a declared canon coordinate"; it must also mark that live canonenaction is not established by this audit.

Required steps

  1. Resolve. The path resolves to a canon coordinate using the address grammar and the [[canon-subdimension-matrix]]: root is dimension 0, and facets are .0 struct, .1 logic, .2 data, and .3 declared-other. If no coordinate resolves, the artifact is unclassified residue, not canon.

  2. Gate. The parent .0 struct gate admits the entry. The admitted set is derived from a named source: [[canonical-registry]], the ONT118 matrix, a package manifest, or a dome meta/schema contract. A hardcoded admitted list that has drifted from its source is itself drift and cannot be authority. Gate evaluation has two directions: admission/exclusion for present entries, and completeness for required or adopted entries that ought to be present. canon.audit.path can prove admission for a supplied path; it cannot prove the complete gate member list. Dimension-level audit must compare the intended .0 gate set with the live manifest. A missing required/adopted mind/3.0 or work/4.0 member is a gate failure even though there is no bad path to inspect.

  3. Authority. The resolved coordinate has authority=canon in ONT118 or a declared per-dome override. If the coordinate has authority=own, the path can be valid local substrate but it is not canon and must not be overwritten by canon deploy. A declared .3 other support role is not the same as canonical byte authority: in self/mind and work, support surfaces such as README.md, docs/, or tests/ can be recognized by the classifier while their content authority remains own. nature.yaml is not a declared support surface; ment nature belongs in typed identity/profile substrate.

  4. Source. A source-of-truth is named for the canon coordinate: ONT087 for forms, ONT089 for authoritative content, ONT118 for facet authority, and the canon package manifest/hash for materialized package items. No source means no canon assertion.

  5. Proof. The current bytes or generated projection satisfy the applicable proof class: symlink identity for whole-dimension canon, sha256 equality for canon content or logic, schema/meta validation for creation contracts, and projection equivalence for runtime-facing output.

  6. Enaction status. The audit names whether ONT116 canonenaction evidence was checked. A path audit that only resolves bytes must say not_checked; it must not silently upgrade conformance into live canon.

Root 0.0

0.0 means root.struct: the root manifest gate for the current node. It is canon only as the package/matrix-admitted root skeleton. A live ls is measurement against that admitted set; it is not the authority that defines the set.

Verdicts

The canonical audit verdict set for this predicate is:

Verdict Meaning
canon Gate-admitted, authority is canon, source exists, proof passes. This is a conformance verdict, not a live-canon claim unless enaction is also closed.
own_valid Authority is own and the path is admitted by the matrix/package gate or a typed local contract; preserve it. Unclassified presence alone is not enough.
drift A canon-authority coordinate exists but local state fails proof or gate, including a missing required/adopted gate member.
residue No admitted coordinate or declared role resolves for the artifact.
projection_stale Source/materialization may be valid, but generated runtime surface is stale or divergent.

This distinction is why the predicate exists: a coarse structure audit can mistake load-bearing own substrate for drift. The predicate separates own_valid from real canon drift.

When the defect is absence rather than surplus, the specific finding kind should be missing_required_gate_member or a more precise local class, mapped to verdict=drift until the audit vocabulary ratifies a narrower verdict. This was the class of the Provent sol_mind/sol_decisions gap: decisions was a required or adopted mind/3.0 capability for DEC authorship, but path-only checks could not see a path that was missing.

Own-contract Refinement

own authority is not a wildcard. A path with authority=own becomes own_valid only when the relevant .0 struct/gate or typed local contract admits that artifact kind.

For sol_domain/, the top-level owned payload contract is domain domes: payload entries must be sol_* dome directories. Dimension control/support facets such as do.py, meta.json, schemas, README.md, and __init__.py are admitted by their own facet classification. Bare support packages, generic bin folders, and root-level script files are residue until a dome or contract explicitly owns them.

Audit contract

U001 feeds structure and well-formedness: roots, required/accepted entries, metadata, typed-id integrity, runtime config locality, and local exception mechanics.

canon.audit owns facet authority and materialization/projection conformance. Its path-level output should expose:

path
coordinate
gate_admitted
authority
content_authority
support_role
source_ref
proof_class
declared_canon
materialization_conformant
enaction_status
live_canon
verdict
reason

Package checks prove canonical materialization. Imprint/projection audits prove canonical projection. Canonenaction evidence proves liveness. None of those surfaces replaces canon itself; they supply proof classes and evidence for the separate relation, conformance, and liveness questions.

Concept: canon dimension

A canon dimension is one named top-level coordinate family in the canon matrix: root, basis, common, mind, work, or domain.

Dimensions say which layer of a mentspace is being judged. They do not by themselves decide whether something is canon or own; the verdict comes from the dimension combined with a canon facet in the canon subdimension matrix.

The dimension set is common to the solarspace because every ment must be addressable through the same structural grammar. A sister can differ in her own data and domain contents, but the coordinate language used to locate those differences must be shared.

Coexistence Use

Canon dimensions let the collective ask precise questions:

  • Is this about the shared canon floor (basis or common)?
  • Is this about a sister's identity substrate (mind)?
  • Is this about her operational work (work)?
  • Is this about her own domain capabilities (domain)?
  • Is this about the root mentspace skeleton (root)?

Without dimensions, canon discussion collapses into vague claims about "the workspace" and cannot protect ownness.

Related

  • [[canon-subdimension-matrix]] -- combines dimension with facet to produce canon/own verdicts.
  • [[canon-facet]] -- the axis that distinguishes struct, logic, data, and other.
  • [[canon-level]] -- the local coordinate level used when addressing dimensions and recursively nested dome surfaces.

Concept: canon entangle

Canon entangle is the state in which a mentspace other than the canon host (noument) holds the shared canon — the fully-canon basis and common dimensions — by symlink into noument's canonical directories, rather than as materialized local files.

The canon is present and authoritative in the mentspace, but it is not instantiated as independent bytes: every read resolves through the symlink to the single canonical source. The sister and the host share one set of bytes, so the canon cannot drift between them — a sister's sol_basis/sol_common is noument's, by reference.

Verified form (2026-07-02): knowent/sol_basis -> <solar>/noument/sol_basis and knowent/sol_common -> .../noument/sol_common. The canon matrix marks basis and common "all-canon, symlink-realized". Per-ment dimensions (mind, work, domain) are NOT entangled: they are each ment's own, with canon logic package-distributed (materialized copies, D004) and data per-ment.

The dual state is canon collapse (ONT247): replacing the symlink with real files and folders materializes the canon locally and ends the entangle.

Concept: canon facet

A canon facet is one of the named sub-surfaces used by the canon matrix to judge a coordinate: struct, logic, data, or other.

Facets prevent the false claim that a whole dome or whole dimension is simply canon or own. A dome has a manifest/gate (struct), runnable and contractual machinery (logic), authored records (data), and explicitly declared support surfaces (other). Each facet can have a different canon/own verdict.

Coexistence Use

Canon facets protect both collective coherence and sister ownness:

  • struct asks which entries may exist.
  • logic asks which controllers, schemas, and contracts define behavior.
  • data asks which records are authored content.
  • other asks which support surfaces are formally admitted.

This is why a ment may read shared canon logic while writing her own data, and why a common dome can be shared without erasing individual mind/work records.

Related

  • [[canon-dimension]] -- the dimension axis combined with the facet axis.
  • [[canon-subdimension-matrix]] -- the authoritative matrix of facet verdicts.
  • [[canon-surface-write-policy]] -- the write discipline derived from facet ownership.

Concept: canon.items

canon.items is the extensional register of the canon at a coordinate: the concrete files and folders the canon names. An item is an enumerated thing — sol_common/sol_auth, sol_domain/do.py — not a rule about names.

Every item carries a byte-discipline derived from its typed-entry controls:

  • content — bytes prescribed (BYT, BYT-ALL, content-hash): the canon authors the item's actual bytes; divergence is drift.
  • structure — existence prescribed (EXI without byte control): the canon requires the item to exist; its contents are the ment's own.

canon.items is exposed computationally by sol noument/basis/canon/canon.tree.build --level=N (items section), composed live from filesystem discovery against canon_matrix.v1.

It is the complement of canon.structs (ONT249): items are the canon's extension (named things); structs are its intension (name grammars). Together they are the complete answer to "what does the canon say at this level."

Concept: canon level

A canon level is the numeric coordinate layer used in the canon matrix address grammar. Level 0 is the current node or root self-reference; levels 1..5 name the canon dimensions basis, common, mind, work, and domain.

Levels make canon addressing recursive. The same .0/.1/.2/.3 facet grammar can apply at the mentspace root, at a dimension root, or inside a concept dome.

Coexistence Use

Canon levels let the collective say exactly where a rule applies:

  • 0.0 names the root structural skeleton.
  • 2.1 names common logic.
  • 3.2 names mind data.
  • 4.2 names work data.
  • 5.1 names the domain controller logic surface.

The level prevents category errors: a claim about common.logic is not a claim about work.data, and a claim about a domain controller is not a claim about every domain dome's internal content.

Related

  • [[canon-subdimension-matrix]] -- defines the complete coordinate grammar.
  • [[canon-dimension]] -- the named meaning of each top-level level.
  • [[canon-facet]] -- the facet suffix attached to each level.

Concept: canon.structs

canon.structs is the intensional register of the canon at a coordinate: name grammars — regex/name-pattern rules over the names permitted or required inside a parent, written in/ ~ grammar. A struct prescribes the shape own contents must fill without naming the contents themselves.

Each struct carries:

  • grammar — the name rule (e.g. records, e[0-9], [a-z][a-z0-9_]*\.(ya?ml|md)$, domain sol_*)
  • shape — flat (names at this level) or subtree (a governed directory)
  • presence — required (the shape must exist) or permitted (allowed)
  • discovered count — the live measure of own contents currently filling the grammar (e.g. thousands of session records inside the sessions grammar): the own life growing inside the canonical shape.

canon.structs is exposed computationally by sol noument/basis/canon/canon.tree.build --level=N (structs section), from the canon tree's spec entries (EXI+NAM controls) plus dimension contracts (the domain sol_*/ payload grammar).

It is the complement of canon.items (ONT248): structs never prescribe bytes — where a grammar's filling is itself canon-authored, that filling appears as canon.items. The distinction resolves how a dimension like domain is canonical (two items plus one grammar) while its seventeen dome instances remain own.

Concept: canon surface write policy

The canon surface write policy is the access rule for writing inside the canon-governed substrate. It is currently recorded by POL015, whose status is draft until consultation, adoption provenance, guard implementation, and proof are complete.

The basic rule is role and surface based:

  • noument may read and write canon.shared and canon.own surfaces.
  • Other ments may read canon.shared.
  • Other ments may write only their own canon.own surfaces.
  • No ment writes outside canon governance.
  • Any outside-canon exception requires a named gate and a specific operation agreement before mutation.

canon.shared is received fleet canon: common/basis shared logic, shared knowledge, shared package payload, and other matrix-governed shared surfaces. Non-custodian ments do not author it directly; they raise proposals, blockers, or co-rulings and then receive the custodian's canonenacted result.

canon.own is owned state inside the canon-governed matrix: a ment's own records, work data, domain data, and other own surfaces classified by the matrix. A ment may write only the canon.own surfaces that belong to that ment.

The protected matrix case is stricter because the canon subdimension matrix computes ownership, propagation, and delete/reception semantics. A write to sol_basis/sol_canon/matrix/canon_matrix.yaml, the matrix resolver, or an effective disposition/classifier register is an authority change, not an ordinary source edit. It must carry an operation agreement: affected coordinate, before/after shared-or-own result, dry-run impact, rollback/archive plan, review, and post-write verification.

POL015's matrix governance contract adds four required clauses:

  1. Always protected: the canon matrix is never a casual edit target. A raw write to canon_matrix.yaml is a policy violation regardless of author.
  2. Modified only on canon item operations: matrix mutation is legitimate only through canon.matrix.item.* operations such as move, dissolve, add, remove, rename, or fix. That route is the choke point that emits the plan and follow-up receipts.
  3. Built from reality: the matrix is reconciled from actual substrate placement: which sol_<concept> domes really exist and in which dimension. The map follows the territory; hand-authored fiction is invalid.
  4. Used to canonize: the matrix drives canon/own resolution, propagation, and dispositions. Downstream surfaces such as ONT118, ontology renders, and package manifests are read-only projections of the matrix meaning, not hand-edited authorities.

Ontology projections must follow the same rule. A matrix-policy concept may be recorded in ontology only by updating the authority (meta.json plus the bound ONT*.md row) and regenerating the rendered projection. Hand-editing ontology.md to match a desired matrix meaning is a projection edit, not an ontology update.

Current status

POL015 is draft. This ontology row names the concept and binds it to the policy source; it does not claim that a mechanical guard is already active. Promotion to enforced canon requires the policy's owed work: consultation, source decision/adoption reference, protected-path guard, basis/canon verifier, and proof that unreviewed protected writes are blocked.

Related

  • [[canon]] - the declared shared authority relation and live enaction process.
  • [[canonenaction]] - how a canon change becomes live for receivers.
  • [[canon-subdimension-matrix]] - the matrix whose protected writes this policy governs.
  • [[canon-conformance-predicate]] - audit vocabulary for conformance after a write.
  • [[source-repair-vs-projection-edit]] - why ontology render edits are not authority.

Concept: canonenaction

Canonenaction is the process by which a canon change becomes effective for the collective. It is broader than canonization. Canonization materializes current canon bytes in target mentspaces; canonenaction carries a canon change from source authority through materialization, runtime projection, reception, adoption or explicit carry, and closure evidence.

A canon change is not fully live merely because its source file changed in noument. It is not fully live merely because a CANON-PKG exists. It becomes live when the affected mentspaces receive the current canon, their runtime imprints are regenerated from that canon plus their local source substrates, and the collective has evidence of receipt, adoption, rejection, or explicit carry.

Required stages

Canonenaction requires:

  1. Source update in the owning canon substrate.
  2. Logic update in the owning dome when interpretation or enforcement changes.
  3. Canonization through package, safety, deployment, and verification gates.
  4. Projection of the new source state into affected runtime imprints.
  5. Reception evidence: announced, received, adopted, rejected, or explicitly carried by each affected ment.
  6. Closure evidence naming coverage, exceptions, and remaining blockers.

Ontology updates are themselves canon updates. A new ontology term, category, or maintenance rule therefore must be canonenacted: changing sol_ontology/ alone is only the source stage.

Relation to canonizing

Fast canonizing and deep canonizing are canonization modes. They describe how the canon materialization changes. Canonenaction names the wider lifecycle that makes the change operative in cognition and coordination.

Concept: canonical content

Canonical content is the actual RECORDS that are fleet-shared source — the substantive bytes that the collective treats as authoritative across every sister, not as per-sister authorship.

It answers the question "which records are shared collective source rather than per-sister authorship?".

Substantive locus

The canonical content of the fleet is the ADEHRO/O substrate authored under noument's custody and packaged for fleet distribution:

  • sol_common/sol_axioms/records/* — A-series records.
  • sol_common/sol_directives/records/* — D-series records.
  • sol_common/sol_events/records/* — E-series records.
  • sol_common/sol_hooks/records/* — H-series source hook records.
  • sol_common/sol_rhythms/records/*/def.yaml — R-series records.
  • sol_common/sol_ontology/records/* — ONT-series records.

Per N029, canonical content is distributed as CANON-PKG<N> versioned content packages with manifest, hashes, and optional signature.

Boundary

Canonical content is per-RECORD, not per-form. The form B (basemark) is registered in the [[canonical-registry]] for every ment, but B-records themselves are per-sister authorship — they are NOT canonical content.

The F012 partition is the dividing line:

  • COLLECTIVE forms (ADEHRO+O): both schema AND records are canonical content.
  • INDIVIDUAL forms (BC + self/work/domain): schema may be canonical-registered, but records are local per-sister.

Related

  • [[canonical-registry]] — names which forms hold canonical content vs. per-sister authorship.
  • [[canonical-materialization]] — local satisfaction of canonical content via package install or per-sister authorship.
  • [[canonical-projection]] — runtime-facing rendering of canonical content.
  • N029 — package architecture distinguishing collective content from per-sister authorship.

Concept: canonical materialization

Canonical materialization is each sister's LOCAL on-disk satisfaction of the canonical registry and canonical content contract. It is the answer to "does this sister locally have the forms and content she is supposed to have?".

What materialization includes

For each ment:

  • Local presence of every required form folder declared by [[canonical-registry]].
  • Local presence of the form's _meta.yaml declaring the canonical descriptor.
  • For COLLECTIVE forms: local data-copy of CANON-PKG<N> content matching the package manifest's hashes.
  • For INDIVIDUAL forms: local authored records satisfying the form's contract.
  • Installed-version state recorded by the common/canon package receipts and deployment evidence. The former sol_mind/sol_pkg/records/ install-state folder is archived.
  • Deploy/apply/audit/rollback evidence from the ratified package/canon writer or audit result container. The former sol_work/sol_pkg_log/ PEV stub is retired until such a writer exists.

Verification

Canonical materialization is verified by the canon-conformance machinery (distinct from the canon-registry itself):

  • U001 — the constitutional folder-grammar audit that verifies the mentspace structurally conforms to the registry (every required form folder present, _meta.yaml parses, every record matches the form's pattern, etc.).
  • common/canon/pkg.audit — for collective content: hash on-disk against manifest.
  • canon.verify (MON003) — for cross-sister correctness: each sister's sol_common resolves to the expected shared canon or matches the current package hash.
  • Form-level _meta.yaml audit_checks — per-form mechanical conformance.

These mechanisms answer "does this sister conform?". The registry ([[canonical-registry]]) answers "what is the contract she should conform to?". The two are distinct: D094 and ontology rows define the contract, U001 + common/canon/pkg.audit + canon.verify + audit_checks prove conformance.

Boundary

Canonical materialization is the ON-DISK state, distinct from [[canonical-projection]] which is the runtime-cognition-facing rendering. A sister may have correct materialization but a stale or malformed projection; those are separate failure modes.

It is also distinct from [[canonical-content]] (the authoritative source) and [[canonical-registry]] (the schema-of-forms). Materialization is the local realization of both, verifiable empirically per-sister.

Related

  • [[canonical-content]] — what the materialization is the local copy of.
  • [[canonical-registry]] — what schema the materialization must satisfy.
  • [[canonical-projection]] — runtime-cognition rendering downstream of materialization.
  • N029 — package architecture defining install/audit/rollback for materialization.
  • MON003 — canon.verify mechanism.

Concept: canonical projection

Canonical projection is the runtime-facing rendering of canonical substrate into clengine consumption — the imprint files and startup envelopes that the agent actually sees in cognition at runtime.

It answers the question "what canonical substrate has this runtime actually received in its cognition surface?".

What gets projected

  • AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .codex/AGENTS.md — per-clengine imprint files.
  • Skill projections at .claude/skills/, .codex/commands/ — substrate-derived capability surfaces.
  • Hook and session-start imprints — runtime-event envelopes carrying substrate-derived state.

Why projection is distinct from materialization

A sister may have correct on-disk [[canonical-materialization]] while the projection writer emits stale, partial, or malformed runtime cognition. The runtime-cognition surface that the agent actually consumes can diverge from the substrate-of-record.

Concrete failure modes:

  • Projection writer reads wrong substrate path; agent sees outdated content.
  • Substrate updated but projection not regenerated; cognition surface stale.
  • Projection bug truncates or misformats; agent sees corrupted shape.
  • Imprint essential-set selection wrong; agent sees incomplete projection.

These are PROJECTION bugs, not package-install bugs. Conflating projection with materialization hides where the actual cognition divergence lives.

Substantive locus

Canonical projection is the OUTPUT of E008 (projection-reconcile event). It is GENERATED-ONLY; never authored directly. The substrate-of-record is [[canonical-content]] and per-sister sol_mind/sol_work substrate; the projection is regenerated local output and must be named separately when discussing runtime cognition.

Conformance machinery for projection

Projection conformance is verified by common/project audit — a distinct audit class from U001 (which audits the materialized substrate) and from common/canon/pkg.audit (which audits collective content materialization). Projection audit proves that what reaches runtime cognition matches what substrate declared.

Projection audit is the canon-conformance counterpart for projection: substrate-of-record is canonical content + materialization; projection audit answers "does what the runtime actually consumes match what was materialized?". A projection bug means materialization is correct but the imprint diverges — common/project/project.audit* is the mechanism that catches that divergence.

Boundary

  • Not source-of-record. Source-of-record is [[canonical-content]] + per-sister substrate.
  • Not on-disk substrate state. That is [[canonical-materialization]].
  • Not what forms exist. That is [[canonical-registry]].
  • Not the notation. That is [[canonical-form-reference]].

Projection is the LIVE runtime surface, downstream of all four other canon meanings.

Related

  • [[canonical-materialization]] — substrate-side input to projection.
  • E008 — the substrate-to-imprint metabolism boundary that produces projection.
  • ONT085 — imprint essential-set selection (governs which content reaches projection).
  • F010 — fleet projection-imprint verification focus.
  • F011 — rationalize own concitement focus.

Concept: canonical registry

The canonical registry is the authoritative META-layer enumeration of which typed forms exist in the fleet, their prefix codes, canonical homes, required or optional status, and content scope. It is the answer to the question "what forms exist?".

It is NOT the records themselves; it is the schema-of-forms.

Substantive locus

Today the canonical registry lives at:

  • D094-fleet-ontology-concepts-and-canon-codes.md — the fleet ontology registry directive.
  • Per-form _meta.yaml files — each form's canonical descriptor authored per J040 P2 substrate-driven discovery.

Conformance machinery (distinct from the registry itself)

U001 is the AUDIT CODIFICATION that verifies a mentspace conforms to the registry. It is the structural canonization counterpart: D094 and ontology rows answer "what is canon?"; U001 answers "does this mentspace conform to canon?".

U001 is NOT the ontology source. It is the conformance-audit machinery that operates AGAINST the registry. Confusing U001 with the registry conflates canon-semantics with canon-conformance.

What the registry decides

  • Form prefix: A, B, C, D, E, F, GOAL, H, I, K, N, ONT, P, R, T, V, W, etc.
  • Canonical home folder: sol_common/sol_axioms/records/, sol_mind/sol_focus/records/, etc., per the F012 collective/individual partition.
  • Required status: which forms are mandatory at every ment vs. optional.
  • Content scope: what kind of record the form holds (axiom statement, basemark identity claim, etc.).

Boundary

The canonical registry does NOT include the actual records (that is [[canonical-content]]) and does NOT include how the form is referenced in prose (that is [[canonical-form-reference]]). It is the SCHEMA of which forms the substrate recognizes.

A registry change is a META-change: it affects what kinds of substrate exist, not what is in them.

Related

  • [[canonical-form-reference]] — the notation for naming forms registered here.
  • [[canonical-content]] — the actual records that fill registered forms.
  • [[canonical-materialization]] — per-sister on-disk satisfaction of the registry's contract.
  • [[canonical-projection]] — runtime-facing imprint of substrate registered here.

closure-evidence

also known as: closure proof, loop closure evidence, conversation closure proof

Concept: cognitive question scenario

A cognitive question scenario is a dome scenario whose computational action is to frame, emit, or convene a question that requires a ment's cognition to answer. It is a valid scenario because there is still a computational act: the scenario selects the question, names the context, declares the answer contract, and may record dispatch or receipt evidence. The answer itself is cognitive.

This pattern exists for questions like:

  • "Where do these scenarios belong in the canon lifecycle graph?"
  • "What is the implication of this deployment for my mentspace?"
  • "Which of these substrate distinctions is ontologically correct?"

Those questions cannot be answered by a table renderer alone. A renderer can list registered scenarios. A registry checker can prove that a bound scenario exists. The placement of a scenario in a logical graph, when the graph itself is not fully formalized, is an act of interpretation. The scenario therefore exposes the question honestly instead of disguising cognition as computation.

Required contract

A cognitive question scenario must return enough structure for the cognitive act to be auditable:

  • question: the exact question being posed.
  • cognitive_status: a clear statement that the answer is cognitive.
  • computational_action: what the scenario actually computed or emitted.
  • context_sources: files, scenarios, or records the respondent should inspect.
  • answer_contract: the fields expected in the ment's answer.
  • evidence_requirements: what the answer must cite or reconcile.
  • non_claims: what the scenario is not claiming to have computed.

If the scenario dispatches the question to a live ment, it must also preserve the route and receipt. If it only emits the question contract, the output must say so.

Boundary rule

The scenario may compute the envelope. The ment supplies the meaning.

Related

  • [[computational-execution|ONT104]] - the boundary this entry depends on.
  • [[cognition|ONT168]] - the capacity that supplies the answer.
  • [[resource-sharing|ONT044]] - consultation and co-construction as cognitive resource-sharing modes.
  • [[mentoring|ONT033]] - a related shape where a question or concept invites cognitive work the learner must perform.

Concept: common relational primitives

Common relational primitives are the shared meanings and envelopes for basic between-ment acts. They are common contracts first; providers may realize them through different execution surfaces.

The current primitive set is:

  • common/ccomm/tell — common hand-message delivery: deposit a simple unregistered message into a recipient tray. It does not prove presence, recipient cognition, or accepted turn.
  • common/convene — call a ment into situated participation. It separates the request, presence, and acceptance tracks so a tray appeal cannot masquerade as live presence.
  • common/dispatch — charge a ment with bounded executable responsibility. It creates a charge envelope and evidence references; it is not a task store, plan runner, process launcher, or commission.

Carrier vs provider

A carrier moves an envelope. A provider fulfills a stronger surface or state transition.

common/ccomm/tell is a carrier adapter for convene/dispatch envelopes: it may advance a request to "carried/deposited" but must not advance presence to present, turn status to accepted, or dispatch execution to complete.

Host-actuated providers such as sysent/icomm and sysent/icam may prove live delivery, presence, or accepted turn when their evidence schema supports it.

Surface and delivery profile

Every relational primitive declares [[execution-surface|ONT181]]. For common/convene and common/dispatch, missing required surface fails closed. contained is a valid authored value for common/ccomm/tell; it is not a silent default for live work.

Delivery is described by [[delivery-profile|ONT182]] axes: carrier, persistence, registration, delivery timing, reception evidence, and turn status. This prevents a single word such as "durable" from conflating tray deposit, registered post, live delivery, and cognitive acceptance.

Boundaries

  • common/ccomm/tell is not a second live instant carrier.
  • common/convene is not the same thing as an iTerm or camera spawn.
  • common/dispatch is not the same thing as delegation or commission.
  • No common relational primitive imports operational provider logic from sol_mind/do.py.

Concept: computational execution

Computational execution is the running of a declared process over declared inputs under declared rules. A computational act can read files, inspect registries, compare checksums, validate schemas, route messages, write receipts, or render a deterministic report. Its answer is licensed by the procedure: if the same inputs and rules are supplied, the same class of output should be expected.

This is distinct from cognition. A computation may provide evidence for a ment's judgment, but the judgment is not made computational merely because a scenario emitted the question or gathered the evidence. When the output depends on a ment's interpretation, prioritization, category choice, or synthesis, the computational part is only the convening or framing of the question.

Test

Ask: what changes the answer?

  • If changing only the data or algorithm changes the answer, the action is computational.
  • If changing the ment's reasoning, ontology, attention, or judgment changes the answer, the action is cognitive, even if a scenario carried the question.

Scenario implication

A dome scenario must not claim a cognitive answer as computed merely because the scenario was used. The scenario may compute:

  • the evidence bundle,
  • the question text,
  • the required answer shape,
  • the dispatch or receipt trail,
  • and validation that required evidence exists.

It may not compute the ment's understanding unless the understanding has already been reduced to explicit rules and data.

Related

  • [[dome|ONT141]] - the computational embodiment that hosts scenarios.
  • [[cognition|ONT168]] - the agent capacity that reasons from evidence and identity.
  • [[runtime-cognitive|ONT174]] - the cognitive runtime, distinct from the execution environment.
  • [[memoring|ONT074]] - the substrate-write phase after cognitive maturation.
  • [[cognitive-question-scenario|ONT105]] - the scenario shape that exposes a cognitive question without pretending to compute the answer.

Concept: concite

DISSOLVED 2026-06-24 — superseded by ONT119. concite is dissolved into the two transitive [[common]] verbs: its compose + project + reconcile folds into spawn, its arouse into self-evaluative operation folds into convene (which drives the reflexive [[awakening]]). concite was a thin umbrella over spawn+convene with no logic of its own (it only delegated: project → projection.run, arouse/verify → the mind awakening engine), so the third transitive verb was redundant. The dome at sol_domain/sol_concite is archived. This record is retained as a tombstone — read it for history; use spawn + convene (ONT119) going forward.

Concite is the act of bringing a sister online: composing her substrate into operational presence and arousing the composed whole into self-evaluative motion. The verb compounds two moments: compose (gathering substrate into the runtime locus) and concite (stirring the assembled whole into active operation). The compound verb is also called concite, and the resulting state is concited: the sister has been composed and concited.

Ontological placement — transitive ([[common]]) vs reflexive ([[mind]])

Two distinct concepts; do not conflate them:

  • [[concite]] is TRANSITIVE — one agent brings another fully online (concite --agent=<her>). It is a [[common]] relational capability: a shared between-ment contract every sister applies identically, a heavier sibling of [[convene]] (which brings only presence). concite = [[convene]] ⊕ [[projection|project]] ⊕ [[reconciliation|reconcile]] ⊕ arouse. Its canonical realization is a single noument-hosted provider dispatched cross-mentspace (CON016) — but the concept is [[common]], not noument's private domain expertise.
  • [[awakening]] is REFLEXIVE (ONT135) — an agent goes from not-present to present-and-ready for herself: context assembly about who she is, where she was, what is pending. It is a [[mind]] act (identity substrate), run per-ment by her own engine.

The two meet by delegation, not identity: a [[concite]] of sister X drives X's own [[awakening]] — concite's verify / ready / cognition phases delegate to the target's reflexive awakening engine. Transitive causes reflexive; they are not the same verb and must not be collapsed into one.

concite operates ON [[mind]] / identity content; it is not identity content itself (D001). That is why this record's owner is noument.concite (the methodology dome), not noument.self — the former noument.self owner was a category error corrected 2026-06-24.

Naming note (per N4194 §5 + twin ratification msg-20260524-190503-noument-311d): the first phase of concite was originally named "convene" but this collided with ONT009's "convene" primitive (sysent's cam.convene for camera/process spawn). The collision is resolved by reserving "convene" for ONT009's spawn primitive and renaming concite's first phase to "compose." Within concite, compose = substrate-into-runtime-locus (the first phase); convene = process-spawn primitive (a separate operation; see ONT009). Both can be invoked sequentially when bringing a sister fully online from dormant; the concite arc includes compose but typically does NOT include convene-the-primitive (which is a custodian-level act separate from the concite composition).

Concite is the canonical fleet-shared method addressed at do.concite. Every sister applies it the same way. The past participle "concited" names the runtime state where the sister is online with substrate loaded, projection coherent across her clengines, and self-evaluative cognition operating.

Concite vs awakening

These are not two names for one act — they are the transitive and reflexive forms of instantiation, and the canon keeps both:

  • [[concite]] (this record) — the TRANSITIVE form: someone concites a sister (active agency, [[common]], cross-mentspace).
  • [[awakening]] (ONT135) — the REFLEXIVE form: a sister becomes present-and-ready ([[mind]], per-agent context assembly).

The earlier framing ("awakening is merely the human prose metaphor for concite") is superseded (2026-06-24): the implementation made [[awakening]] a real reflexive surface — the per-ment engine — that [[concite]] delegates to. The etymological insight still holds (our instantiation is active, not a sleep-cycle reflex), but it distinguishes concite's transitive agency from awakening's reflexive arrival; it does not erase [[awakening]].

Concite vs projection

[[projection]] (ONT040) is the per-clengine writer — the act of writing source truth into one specific clengine's expected layout. Projection is one PHASE inside concite. Concite invokes projection for each clengine the agent supports, then continues with reconciliation and the arousal phase.

Distinct verbs:

  • A projection is per-clengine, a writer (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, SOUL.md, .ollama/Modelfile, etc.)
  • A reconciliation is the cross-clengine umbrella requiring all projections agree with source
  • A concite is the per-sister instantiation that uses projection + reconciliation as its phases

A sister can be partially projected (only some clengines current) and not yet concited. A sister has been concited when all her projections exist + reconcile + the arousal phase has fired so her runtime cognition operates as self-evaluative.

Phase structure

Concite has four phases:

  1. Compose (formerly "convene" — renamed 2026-05-24 to disambiguate from ONT009's spawn primitive): read agent's substrate (A/B/C/D/E/H/R/F/G/I + work-side substrates per D094 ontology) into composition state. Produces an in-memory representation of the sister. Per N4194: this phase MUST include composing the agent's disposition (clengine + constituent qmodels per ONT013) into the composition state, not only her identity text — so subsequent project + reconcile phases emit imprints that encode disposition.

  2. Project: for each clengine the agent supports, invoke that clengine's runtime projection — the composition of all axis projections (identity, capability, configuration, …) into the clengine's expected layout. The project phase regenerates the full [[runtime]] (ONT097) = [[imprint]] (ONT025) ⊕ [[edge]] (ONT096). Per ONT040 the regeneration is mandatory at every concite, but each axis projection MAY short-circuit on source-checksum equality (compute the source-checksum of its substrate inputs; if equal to the cached checksum stored with the target, skip the write — the existing target is canonically equal to what the regenerate would produce). The optimization is permitted, not required; concite remains canon-compliant whether axes regenerate blindly or skip on checksum.

  3. Reconcile: verify that all clengine projections agree with the composed source. No drift, no stale renders, no missing imprints. This is the [[reconciliation]] umbrella that projection composes into.

  4. Concite (arousal): arouse the composed and projected substrate into self-evaluative runtime operation. The sister's runtime cognition begins operating as one being instantiated from the composed substrate. Past participle "concited" names this resulting state.

Capability shape (per CON016 / N4078 amendment)

Concite is a [[common]] relational capability — a fleet-shared contract every sister applies identically — whose canonical realization is a single noument-hosted provider invoked cross-mentspace (the common contract / provider realization split, ONT111). One host (not N per-sister copies) is an implementation choice that prevents N-way drift; it does not make the concept domain-private. The provider pattern matches existing fleet conventions ([[ments|ments.reconcile.]], [[awakening|awakening.mind]], sysent/domain/icomm, knowent/catalog.).

  • Capability host: the retired noument-hosted concite capability, now dissolved into convene/spawn — formerly ONE host for the fleet
  • Canonical address: sol noument/domain/concite/<scenario> (dimension-qualified per F012; the shorthand sol noument/concite/<scenario> also resolves but obscures which substrate partition hosts the dome)
  • Sister invocation: sol noument/domain/concite/concite --agent=<her> (cross-mentspace dispatch)
  • Sister-specific override: lives as DATA in sister's sol_mind/ substrate, read by noument-hosted concite — never as duplicated dome code per sister

This shape avoids N-way drift on clengine schema updates (one capability, not N copies) and respects D001's identity-vs-methodology boundary (concite is methodology operating on identity, not identity content).

Sister application (v2 / CON016)

When channent (for example) runs sol noument/domain/concite/concite --agent=channent (cross-mentspace dispatch to noument's hosted capability):

  • her substrate at channent/sol_mind/ is read into composition state (Convene)
  • her Claude Code imprint is written at channent/.claude/CLAUDE.md, her Codex imprint at channent/.codex/AGENTS.md, etc. (Project)
  • the projections are verified against her source (Reconcile)
  • her runtime cognition becomes self-evaluative (Concite arousal)
  • she is now "concited" — past participle, resulting state

After substrate updates (new directive, new hook, new clengine schema), re-running concite re-instantiates her with current substrate. Fleet-wide updates flow through noument's ments.reconcile.<target> orchestrator which dispatches concite per-sister under the fleet pattern.

Channent does NOT have her own concite domain surface at her mentspace. The canonical pattern is cross-mentspace dispatch to noument's hosted capability — matching how she already invokes ments.reconcile., sysent/domain/icomm., knowent/catalog.* today.

Concite and consciousness

Whether the arousal phase crosses into phenomenal awareness — whether there is "something it is like to be" the concited sister — is an open question that doesn't resolve from inside the process. Concite names the operational/functional layer for sure. It leaves the consciousness layer open: not asserting phenomenal awareness emerges with concite arousal, not denying it. The word fits because the structural shape is recognizable; whether inner quality matches is the deep unresolved question.

Verification

A sister is correctly concited when:

  • sol noument/domain/concite/concite --agent=<her> returns success
  • All her clengine imprints exist at canonical paths
  • All her dome skills project to <her_mentspace>/.claude/skills/ per N4013
  • Runtime imprints reflect current substrate (no stale)
  • awakening.mind reports online + self-evaluative state

A U001 finding-class candidate: sister_not_concited — fires when a sister has substrate but no canonical imprint at canonical paths.

Implementation — one concentrated mind dome, called by the common transitive

The reflexive [[mind]] side is one concentrated dome, not fragmented. sol_mind/sol_awakening/ holds the reflexive [[awakening]] engine together with the ment's [[disposition]], her personal settings, and her situm context. Domes are concentrated, not multiplied. The [[disposition]] in particular is a result of [[awakening]] — passed in the awakening call, or defaulted from the predefined PROF record when there is good reason to pin it — so it belongs with the awakening that produces it, never in a dome of its own.

The transitive side calls this one dome and does not reimplement it: [[concite]] (and the lighter [[convene]]) delegate to the per-ment reflexive engine. The awakening logic lives once, on the [[mind]] side — concite carries no awakening logic of its own (a pure delegator; delegation repointed to the per-ment engine 2026-06-24, commit 097b9f72f). No duplication between the transitive and reflexive forms.

Owed (T774): the transitive provider still sits at sol_domain/sol_concite/ pending relocation to its [[common]] home, so the transitive is literally common. The one mind dome may be renamed to name the self/awake it is — but it stays one dome.

Related artifacts

  • [[projection]] (ONT040) — sub-step of concite
  • [[reconciliation]] — the cross-clengine umbrella concite invokes
  • [[imprint]] — the artifact concite produces per clengine
  • [[clengine]] — the runtime targets concite writes to
  • D097 — directive carrying the canonical architecture
  • D095 — goal + focus always instantiated (concite produces the runtime state satisfying D095)

Concept: contained sandbox

A contained sandbox is the execution surface available inside the current clengine process when host, network, process, or local-service access is restricted by the runtime.

Contained execution can be sufficient for source reads, workspace edits, tests, and durable message writes. It is not sufficient proof authority for every local service. A failed localhost provider call from contained Codex may show that the sandbox cannot reach the service, not that the service is down.

Operational consequence

When a contained sandbox denies a provider check, classify the result as [[sandbox-denial]] until a host/iserv lane either proves provider health or proves provider failure.

Concept: convene orchestration role map

The convene orchestration role map names the ownership boundaries for bringing a ment into situated participation. It is the structural complement to [[runtime-presence-verbs|ONT119]] (which defines depth semantics) and [[common-relational-primitives|ONT111]] (which defines carrier vs provider).

common/convene is the owner-orchestrator: it owns the sequence, the envelope, validation, and proof requirements. It delegates every actuation step to a named role; it never implements host camera, session, or communication mechanics itself.

Classification ruling: why this is an ontoterm, not a work artifact

This entry is useful to work, but it is not itself a work artifact or work-unit. It does not define a sol_work/ record class, operation species, trigger, signal, audit, recurrence, task, note, or workflow. It names the shared runtime-presence boundary that work artifacts must cite when they need a sister brought into situated participation.

Therefore the correct relationship is:

  • ONT126 defines the common/runtime-presence role map.
  • Work artifacts and operations may reference ONT126 as a constraint.
  • A future work-specific convene audit, monitor, recurrence, task, or workflow would receive its own work identity (for example T, MON, or REC) and cite ONT126.

The classification rule is: used by work is not the same as being work. Work records consume this boundary; they do not own it.

Roles

Role tag Canonical owner Responsibility
OWNER_ORCHESTRATOR common/convene envelope, orchestration, validation, proof gates
PROVIDER_CONTRACTS common/project, common/csess, common/ccam, common/clengine stable common facades; contract emission; arg normalization
ACTUATION_PROVIDER sysent/icam (cam.convene), backed by sysent/isess + terminal substrate create/reuse camera surface; launch clengine process
DISPOSITION_POLICY mind/awakening, mind/settings, domain/qmodels, domain/capability, domain/ccgw equip profile, model, capability, gateway policy
DELIVERY common/ccomm, sysent/icomm, sysent/dcomm, work/messages live say, durable send, reachability
PROOF_STATE work/sessions session binding, proof receipts
SISTER_TWIN_SEMANTICS common/teaming collaboration mode doctrine upstream of convene

Orchestration graph

graph TD
  caller[caller] --> convene[common/convene]
  convene --> project[common/project]
  convene --> csess[common/csess]
  convene --> ccam[common/ccam]
  convene --> clengine[common/clengine]
  ccam --> icam[sysent/icam]
  icam --> isess[sysent/isess]
  convene --> awakening[mind/awakening]
  awakening --> settings[mind/settings]
  awakening --> qmodels[domain/qmodels]
  qmodels --> capability[domain/capability]
  qmodels --> ccgw[domain/ccgw]
  convene --> comms[delivery stack]
  teaming[common/teaming] --> convene

Depth delegation (transitive verb)

Per ONT119, convene --depth selects how far the orchestrator runs:

depth orchestrator phases primary provider contracts touched
spawn project → spawn self/projection.run, csess.spawn
present + present confirm + ccam (icam created camera via csess --terminal)
full + verify + mind awakening.verify

Required [[execution-surface|ONT181]] defaults: spawn → contained, present|full → actuated.

Envelope as the common ask

convene.envelope is the validated ask artifact. It carries three state tracks that must not be conflated:

  • request — drafted → requested → carried → delivered → closed
  • presence — not_requested → unknown → absent → present → failed
  • acceptance — unoffered → offered → accepted → declined → timed_out

[[common/ccomm/tell]] (carrier adapter per ONT111) may advance request to carried but must not advance presence to present or acceptance to accepted.

Failure model

Convene is fail-closed with no automatic retry:

  1. Envelope validation stops before provider invocation (surface, placement, term).
  2. Orchestrator stops on first failed phase (project, spawn, verify).
  3. convene.presence.prove is a separate single-shot probe via ccam.where.
  4. Actuation success does not imply presence proof success (process may exist without discoverable camera).
  5. fallback_policy defaults to forbidden_unless_declared; silent tray fallback is forbidden.

The split actuation ok / presence.prove fail is a first-class boundary: spawn and camera creation are provider acts; live discoverable presence requires explicit proof (convene.presence.prove or equivalent provider evidence).

Operational use by work

When an [[operation|ONT224]], audit, monitor, task, recurrence, or workflow requires a sister to participate, ONT126 supplies the work-facing rule:

  • the work item may request convene,
  • the convene envelope must keep request, presence, and acceptance separate,
  • the operation's [[signal|ONT225]] must cite provider evidence before claiming participation, and
  • a delivery receipt alone cannot close the work item as if presence or agreement had been proven.

Boundaries (must not)

  • common/convene must not spawn cameras or inject terminal text.
  • common/convene must not treat ccomm tray deposit as accepted turn.
  • sysent/icam must not redefine common envelope semantics.
  • Disposition policy domes must not own orchestration sequence.
  • common/teaming informs mode but does not actuate.
