conversation-alignment

<!-- ontology-id: ONT190@noument concept=conversation-alignment -->

<!-- source: producer=work@noument/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: interaction alignment, goal-state alignment, conversation realignment

Conversation-alignment is the operation of comparing the extracted conversation state against the conversation-goal and correcting the next action when they diverge. It turns a passive state reading into an operational contract: what must be said, asked, acknowledged, produced, or polled next.

See also: conversation-goal · conversation-state-extraction · operational-integrity

conversation-continuity-contract

<!-- ontology-id: ONT191@noument concept=conversation-continuity-contract -->

<!-- source: producer=work@noument/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: continuity contract, interaction continuity contract, collaboration continuity contract

A conversation-continuity-contract is the operational binding that prevents a live collaboration from dissolving after messages are sent. It records the goal, extracted state, alignment, next movement, poll trigger, and closure evidence required to keep the loop active until it closes.

See also: conversation-goal · conversation-state-extraction · conversation-alignment · operational-integrity

conversation-goal

<!-- ontology-id: ONT188@noument concept=conversation-goal -->

<!-- source: producer=work@noument/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: interaction goal, conversation objective, goal-state

A conversation-goal is the intended work-state a conversation is meant to produce. It is not the topic or transcript title; it is the target condition that lets the participants decide whether the exchange has advanced, drifted, or closed.

See also: operational-integrity

conversation-state-extraction

<!-- ontology-id: ONT189@noument concept=conversation-state-extraction -->

<!-- source: producer=work@noument/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: conversation state, interaction-state extraction, message-state extraction, CSE

Conversation-state extraction is the operation of deriving the current actionable state of an interaction from its message and exchange history. It is not transcript summary: it identifies what request is still live, which later messages supersede or narrow earlier ones, what delivery or action evidence exists, what remains blocked, and what communication closes the loop.

See also: conversation-goal · conversation-alignment · canonical-message · operational-integrity

cron

<!-- ontology-id: ONT215@noument concept=cron -->

<!-- source: producer=work@noument/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: sol_crons, sol_work/sol_crons, CRON

cron is the sol_work/sol_crons/ concept for Scheduled work substrate (CRON-typed). Each schedule has THREE record-classes: def: scheduler-neutral definition of recurrent work proj: platform-specific projection derived from the definition obs: runtime observation or evidence (snapshot from a runtime surface)

The model is layered: recurrent obligation (sol_domain/sol_ag/AGR*.yaml) → schedule definition (sol_work/sol_crons/records/CRON###-def-<slug>.yaml) → platform projection (sol_work/sol_crons/records/CRON###-proj-launchd-<slug>.plist) → runtime projection (~/Library/LaunchAgents/<plist Label>.plist) → runtime observation (sol_work/sol_crons/records/CRON###-obs-<surface>-<date>/) It uses identifier prefix CRON and filename form CRON{nnn}-def-{slug}.yaml.

Evidence refs: sol_work.do.WORK_CONCEPTS · sol_work.do.WorkConcept

See also: sol-work · identifier-prefixes

Concept: deep canonizing

Deep canonizing is the full-resolution form of canon update. It is used when the custodian must not only distribute current canon bytes, but also determine what the canon should be, what target material must be preserved, what old material must be archived, and what health evidence proves the collective is safe after mutation.

Deep canonizing treats canonization as a program, not a file operation. It may include ontology decisions, directive conciliation, schema correction, source repair, pack generation, yeval, target-specific migration, archive-first supersede, projection regeneration, U001 warning qualification, runtime verification, and final disposition.

Required shape

A deep canonizing run must answer four classes of question:

  1. Source truth: what belongs in the canon source, and why?
  2. Package truth: does the generated pack exactly match the expected canon?
  3. Target truth: what will be added, updated, replaced, deleted, archived, or migrated in each mentspace?
  4. Health truth: after mutation, is the target exact, structurally healthy, runtime-healthy, and dispositioned?

The answer may include computational evidence and cognitive judgments. The computational parts include inventory generation, checksum comparison, yeval, pack verification, archive receipt verification, and post-apply exactness. The cognitive parts include ontology placement, authorship classification, conciliation, waiver decisions, owner routing, and the decision that a target-specific risk is safe enough to supersede.

Typical use

Deep canonizing is required for:

  • moving or dissolving domes,
  • changing canonical definitions or ontology categories,
  • adding ADEHRO material whose effect is not bounded to one path,
  • resolving duplicated or self-local directives into common canon,
  • migrating messages, identity, profile, or authored work data,
  • archiving old paths,
  • changing generators for runtime projections,
  • or closing U001 fleet health rather than only pack exactness.

Archive rule

Deep canonizing never removes as its first act. It archives before removal, records receipt, verifies rollback sufficiency, and only then allows a target path to disappear from the active house. This is what distinguishes custodial supersede from destructive cleanup.

Scenario binding

Deep canonizing is not one scenario yet. It is a trip family across canon, collective, ontology, projection, hooks, audits, notes, and per-owner question scenarios. Its current operational center is the P033 class of collective canon refresh and health verification.

Relation to fast canonizing

Fast canonizing is a special case inside the larger deep discipline. A fast run is valid only while the impact remains bounded and non-destructive. The moment a run exposes target-specific authored material, delete/archive decisions, runtime-generator defects, or schema uncertainty, the work has become deep canonizing.

Related

  • [[fast-canonizing|ONT106]] - the bounded-resolution form.
  • [[computational-execution|ONT104]] - the computable evidence surface.
  • [[cognitive-question-scenario|ONT105]] - the honest way to convene judgment when the answer is not reducible to the checker.
  • [[canonical-content|ONT089]] - source truth.
  • [[canonical-materialization|ONT090]] - target truth.
  • [[package-manifest|ONT093]] - pack truth.
  • [[canonenaction|ONT116]] - the wider source-to-runtime adoption lifecycle.

Concept: delivery profile

A delivery profile is the decomposed execution-contract description of what a communication or coordination scenario actually did and what evidence it has.

Canonical property spelling: delivery_profile.

The older spelling delivery_contract may remain as a named profile assembled from the axes below. It is not itself a primitive axis. A single value such as durable cannot honestly encode persistence, registration, live timing, and recipient cognition at once.

Delivery profile is orthogonal to [[execution-surface|ONT181]]. Execution surface names the authority required to attempt the operation; delivery profile names the carrier/result evidence. A scenario can require actuated execution surface because it pastes into a live terminal, while its profile records whether the result was only delivered to a surface, acknowledged, or accepted as a turn.

Axes

Axis Values Meaning
carrier carrier/provider id The mechanism that moved the envelope, such as common/ccomm, sysent/icomm, or registered post.
persistence ephemeral, persisted Whether a durable local record was written.
registration unregistered, registered Whether the record participates in the registered work-message/post substrate.
delivery_timing deposit_now, deferred, live_now Whether the send deposited now, waits for later pickup, or reached a live surface now.
reception_evidence none, deposited, delivered_to_surface, ack, reply What evidence exists that the recipient received or responded.
turn_status none, offered, accepted, declined, completed Whether a cognitive turn was offered/accepted/completed.

Defaults and fallback

There is no universal default delivery profile for relational primitives. A scenario must declare its profile axes, or name a profile whose axes are defined.

Fallback is forbidden unless the scenario declares it. This preserves live communication semantics: if a caller asks for live delivery or accepted-turn coordination, tray or registered fallback is a typed blocked/degraded result, not full success.

Communication mapping

  • common/ccomm/tell: execution_surface=contained, carrier=common/ccomm, persistence=persisted, registration=unregistered, delivery_timing=deposit_now, reception_evidence=deposited, turn_status=none.
  • sysent/domain/icomm/reach: execution_surface=observed, profile depends on whether it observed a live route, deposited a declared fallback, or both.
  • sysent/domain/icomm/tell: usually execution_surface=actuated when the selected path can use live tty injection or autowake; profile must record carrier, live delivery evidence, and whether any durable record was also written.
  • sysent/domain/icomm/ask or sysent/domain/icomm/convene with a real answer requirement: turn_status=accepted only with ack/reply/accepted-turn proof.

These examples classify contracts; the owning domes (sysent for icomm, common/ccomm for ccomm) author the scenario metadata.

Runtime resolution

At runtime, the dome runner evaluates separate questions:

  1. Does the current clengine/session capability satisfy the declared execution_surface?
  2. Which carrier/provider moved the envelope?
  3. What persistence, registration, timing, reception, and turn evidence exists?
  4. Does that evidence satisfy the named profile or caller requirement?

A Codex sandbox may satisfy contained execution but not actuated execution. That is a surface-capability result. A live paste into a busy pane may satisfy instant transport but not accepted_turn. That is a delivery-contract result.

Both axes must be reported distinctly so the system can distinguish route failure, actuator failure, receiver-readiness failure, and cognitive-response failure.

Concept: deploy

Deploy is the propagation of a canon change from its single canonical host (noument's sol_common/ and sol_mind/ logic) out to every mentspace that mirrors it, so the fleet's runtime matches the canonical source. Deploy operates on shared canon logic, never on per-ment records (those stay individual — D003/D028).

Deploy is distinct from its neighbours, by scope:

  • [[projection]] (ONT040) writes one ment's source into one clengine's runtime edge (CLAUDE.md, skills) — within a mentspace.
  • [[concite]] (ONT084) brings one sister online (compose → project → reconcile → arouse) — one ment.
  • deploy propagates a canon-logic change across all mentspaces — between ments. A canon change is not complete when noument's source changes; it is complete when deployed.

Deploy of a canon dome rename (the reference case)

When a canon mind/common dome is renamed — e.g. sol_mind/sol_profile → sol_mind/sol_awakening (2026-06-24) — the rename is canonical only at noument. Every sister mirrors that dome's logic by symlink to noument's items. Deploy is therefore, for each mentspace:

  1. Rename the dome folder sol_mind/<old> → sol_mind/<new>.
  2. Repoint the logic symlinks — do.py, meta.json, schemas (and any other mirrored logic file) — to noument's NEW canonical path (…/noument/sol_mind/<new>/…). These remain symlinks to noument items: the logic is canon, hosted once (D078).
  3. Leave per-ment records in place — records/ (PROF*, …), domes/, scripts/ are real per-ment data; they ride along with the folder rename and are never repointed.

Done condition: zero dangling symlinks fleet-wide; each sister's dome resolves to noument's renamed canonical logic and reads her own records. A "rename" that touches noument only — skipping the mentspaces — leaves every sister's symlink dangling: the rename is not deployed until the fleet is repointed, and the break surfaces silently only when a sister next loads the dome.

Mechanism status (honest)

sol_common/ canon deploys via collective.deploy.* (D078). A canon mind-dome rename has no dedicated deploy tool yet: the 2026-06-24 awakening rename was deployed by direct folder-rename + symlink-repoint across 43 mentspaces (0 dangling after; records preserved). A reusable deploy scenario for dome renames is owed work — until it exists, a canon dome rename carries the manual deploy as part of the A040 (identify → resolve) obligation that makes the change complete.

Concept: do-es-notation

Title: retired do. / es. shorthand.

do. / es. notation is retired. It is retained only so old notes, directives, and ontology rows can be interpreted historically. It is not the current canonical notation, not an invocation grammar, and not a proof that a path or scenario is live.

It is also not a canonization grammar. To say what is canon, first identify the actual filesystem path or Sol address, then classify it through the [[canon-subdimension-matrix]] ([[ONT118]]) by dimension × facet, for example mind.data (3.2) or common.logic (2.1).

Current references must use one of two concrete forms:

  1. Solar filesystem paths for bytes on disk, for example sol_common/sol_ontology/do.py, sol_common/sol_ontology/meta.json, and sol_common/sol_ontology/records/*.
  2. Sol addresses for invocation, for example common/ontology/o.status or the full command sol noument/common/ontology/o.status.

These forms must not be mixed. Examples of invalid mixed notation include sol_mind/directives, mind/sol_directives, and sol_common/ontology/o.status.

For dome substrate, name the actual dome folder and its actual contract or record collection:

  • dome logic: sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/do.py
  • substrate contract: sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/meta.json
  • record collection: sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/records/*

Historical rows may still mention legacy flat entrypoint names or legacy metadata files; those are legacy compatibility references unless a current row or live filesystem check explicitly proves otherwise.

The replacement ontology concept is [[canonical-form-reference]] ([[ONT088]]), which now distinguishes filesystem-path references from Sol-address references. ONT088 names reference forms; ONT118 names canon/own classification.

  • [[dome]] -- canonical executable interface.
  • [[identifier-prefixes]] -- numbered entity prefixes.
  • [[conformance-triplet]] -- specification/operator/evidence alignment.
  • [[canon-subdimension-matrix]] -- dimension/facet canon classification.
  • D094 -- fleet ontology of typed substrate concepts and canon codes.

Concept: do.x

A do.x is a dome-owned operational x-action — a named review / monitor / probe verb that a dome exposes as a first-class scenario, composed from lower-level dome primitives, rather than performed as an external ad-hoc procedure. The x is the operational verb (review, monitor, probe, audit, ...).

A do.x scenario declares, as part of its contract:

  • side-effect class — read-only by default; repair/apply only when explicitly declared.
  • arguments — stable, named inputs.
  • output schema — a versioned result shape (e.g. cam.review.v1).
  • evidence fields — what the action observed, so the result is verifiable.

Examples

  • cam.review --present --format=json — composes cams -> termion -> header/title -> pane -> composer -> exposer -> sister-on-cam -> ongoing task -> communication readiness -> disposition, returning cam.review.v1.
  • cam.monitor --present --interval=30 --format=jsonl — a bounded/streaming read-only observation stream (cam.monitor.event.v1).

Invariants

  • read-only by default — a do.x is observational unless it explicitly declares a repair/apply side-effect class.
  • composed, not ad-hoc — a do.x is built from the dome's own primitives and exposed as a scenario, never an external procedure scraping substrate.
  • schema + evidence — a do.x returns a versioned output schema with evidence fields, so its result is verifiable (not prose).

Naming vs convention

This entry names the concept (the do.x scenario-class noun, referenceable like [[termion]]/[[runtime-presence-verbs]]). The authoring convention (the read-only-default

  • declared side-effect class + stable args + output schema + evidence-fields contract) is formalized as a directive — concept here, rule there. cam.review / cam.monitor are the canonical motivating instances.

Concept: dome-grammar

Dome grammar is the direct source-level invocation form for executing a dome's own do.py file.

It names bytes on disk first, then names the dome scenario with -x.

python <solar-root>/<ment>/sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/do.py -x <scenario> [--arg=value ...]

Example:

python sol_domain/sol_dcomm/do.py -x dcomm.rationalize --agent=provent --all_cameras --dry_run --format=json

Semantics

  • <solar-root>/<ment>/sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/do.py is a Solar filesystem path to a specific dome logic file.
  • -x <scenario> selects a scenario key registered by that do.py.
  • Remaining flags are parsed by the dome scenario, not by the Sol address router.

Boundary

Dome grammar is not Sol grammar. It does not prove that a routed Sol address resolves, and it must not be written as a mixed form such as sol_domain/sol_dcomm/dcomm.rationalize.

Use dome grammar for implementation debugging, owner-local proving, and checking what a specific do.py exposes. Use [[sol-grammar]] when the claim is about the collective routed invocation surface.

Equivalence rule

Dome grammar and Sol grammar can have the same intended domain purpose: invoke a named scenario with a set of arguments. Their intended domain outcome should be route-invariant when both calls correspond to the same declared scenario contract.

The declared scenario contract is the reference of truth, not the direct do.py -x call. Direct execution is itself a prover that can be polluted by caller cwd, environment, import path, or local substrate state.

Sol grammar deliberately adds canonical envelope concerns that direct dome grammar does not prove: address registration, route resolution, locality, sender/camera context, argument normalization, policy, and projected skill surface. Those additions are intended route behavior. They are bugs only when they silently produce an observable, undeclared, unintended change to the domain outcome.

Proof is asymmetric:

  • direct dome grammar passing proves implementation executability only;
  • Sol grammar passing proves implementation execution plus canonical routed availability;
  • dome-passes/Sol-fails is a route, registration, locality, or projection availability defect unless the failure is explicitly declared by contract.

The enforcement shape is a differential route-invariance prover: run the corresponding direct and Sol calls, compare the domain outcome modulo the declared envelope and declared transformations, and report both both-pass-but-differ and dome-pass/Sol-fail cases.

That prover must not naively double-run effectful scenarios. Its first safe scope is read/pure scenarios. Effectful scenarios require an explicit dry-run, idempotency, worktree-isolation, or equivalent replay contract; otherwise the prover must refuse instead of manufacturing a comparison.

Related

  • [[dome]] -- executable interface owned by a substrate folder.
  • [[scenario]] -- the callable key selected by -x.
  • [[canonical-form-reference]] -- the non-mixing rule for filesystem paths and invocation addresses.
  • [[sol-grammar]] -- routed invocation through the sol command.

Concept: dome substrate

The data half of the dome + dome-substrate pair. The typed records authored to disk under the current single-folder dome layout. Operated on by the dome (do.py logic).

The pair

Every typed concept in the framework is represented as a conceptual pair. In the current filesystem layout, both halves usually live in one dome folder:

Half Filesystem Role
dome sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/do.py LOGIC — scenarios, handlers, operations
dome substrate sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/meta.json, records/, and declared support data DATA — contracts and typed records the dome operates on

Either half without the other is incomplete:

  • A dome without substrate is logic with no records to operate on.
  • A substrate without a dome is data with no operator (orphan; see ONT068 dome-support-folder).

Membership rule

A folder is a dome substrate IFF:

  1. It is a declared dome folder under a layer, usually sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/.
  2. It has a meta.json contract or equivalent declared component metadata.
  3. It contains typed records following the concept's typed-id pattern (A###-*.yaml, D###-*.yaml, GOAL###-*.yaml, T###-*.{yaml,md}, etc.) when the form is record-backed.
  4. Its records/support data are operated by the paired dome logic (do.py) or by a declared owner surface.

What is NOT dome substrate

  • The dome logic itself (do.py) — that is logic. The substrate is the contract, records, and support data the logic operates on.
  • Runtime artifacts (.claude/CLAUDE.md, .claude/settings.json, .claude/skills/*) — these are PRODUCED FROM substrate by projection. They are runtime artifacts, not substrate. See ONT025 imprint, ONT096 edge.
  • Logs and traces (_logs/events/E###/, _logs/hooks/) — evidence captured AT runtime events. Evidence, not substrate.
  • Cache and ephemeral state (_state/*, _active.json, *.pyc) — runtime scratch. Not authored, not canonical.
  • Archive (_archive/*) — was substrate; preserved as historical record; no longer active.
  • Repository metadata (.git/, .gitignore) — VCS and tooling, not framework substrate.

Loose usage to avoid

The bare word "substrate" was previously used to mean three different things in noument's substrate-of-record:

  1. Substrate (strict, this ONT179): the dome's declared contract, typed records, and support data, excluding the executing logic.
  2. Substrate-of-record (looser): the broader truth-bearing artifact set — records + dome code + matrix + metadata. The truth-bearing canon.
  3. Canonical artifact (loosest): anything authored and persistent.

Going forward, "substrate" defaults to meaning (1). When meaning (2) or (3) is intended, use the explicit term ("substrate-of-record" / "canonical artifact").

Dimensional placement

Dome substrates live in all four dimensions:

Dimension Example substrates
sol_common/ sol_axioms/, sol_directives/, sol_hooks/, sol_ontology/ — collective canon (fleet-shared records)
sol_mind/ sol_basemarks/, sol_commitments/, sol_identity/, sol_knowing/, sol_memoring/, sol_visuals/ — per-sister identity
sol_work/ sol_notes/, sol_tasks/, sol_projects/, sol_sessions/, sol_audits/, etc. — per-sister operational state
sol_domain/ per-sister distinctive substrates (for example mentspace or capability domain data)

Per the capacity/content framework (see N4204 ENSHRINED FRAMEWORK):

  • common substrate = capacity shared + content shared (Y/Y)
  • self/work substrate = capacity shared (logic packaged) + content individual (Y/N)
  • domain substrate = capacity distinctive per sister (N/N)

Why this distinction matters

The dome/substrate separation is load-bearing for several disciplines:

  • D004 (package vs dome separation): code lives in the dome OR a sibling pkg; implementation code in records-only substrate content is a category-error violation (see N4204 §s9b for the 6 violation cases this session).
  • Custody pairing (per doment msg-20260525-112949): a dome scenario must operate only on its own mentspace substrate; cross-mentspace writes require SOURCE-READ pattern.
  • F006 packaging: pkg distribution applies to logic (code in dome or sibling pkg). Substrate is per-sister content; pkg deploys the operator, not the data.
  • Matrix authority (U001): the control_matrix declares the canonical do+substrate shape per dimension; deviations are findings.

Cross-references

  • ONT141 dome — defines the do.py logic half of the pair.
  • ONT088 canonical form reference — current rule for naming filesystem bytes versus executable Sol addresses without mixing syntaxes.
  • ONT176 do/es notation — retired shorthand retained only for interpreting historical references.
  • ONT085 imprint-essential-set-selection — substrate items selected for imprint composition.
  • N4204 ENSHRINED FRAMEWORK — capacity/content × 4-dimension × 4-destination classification grounds why this distinction matters.

Concept: edge

Edge is the behavioral surface of a clengine's runtime — the set of files in the runtime layout that determine what the agent CAN DO and HOW she behaves, distinct from the identity file (the [[imprint]]) that determines who she IS.

Closure rule

A file at <mentspace>/.claude/<X> is part of edge IFF both:

  1. The clengine officially consumes it (Claude Code reads it at session start or during tool dispatch); AND
  2. There exists a substrate-of-record record + a noument-side projection writer that produces it (a four-property ONT040 projection: source record, generator scenario, target path, reconciliation check).

The two clauses are conjunctive. A file the clengine reads but noument does not project (e.g., Anthropic-shipped slash commands) is not edge — reconciliation has nothing to verify. A file noument projects but the clengine does not read (e.g., mentspace.json) is not edge — it is co-located runtime state. Edge is precisely the intersection.

Edge membership for Claude Code

Under the closure rule, the Claude-runtime edge set is:

Edge artifact SoR Generator Edge member
.claude/skills/ dome scenario registrations from folder-form dome entrypoints skills.project (sol_common/sol_skills/do.py) ✓
.claude/agents/ NOUMENTS registry (sol_mind/sol_identity/records/) ments.reconcile.claude sync_noument_to_claude ✓
.claude/settings.json SET1-<agent>-runtime.yaml settings.project ✓
.claude/hooks/ (scripts referenced from settings) H###-*.{yaml,sh,py} ments.reconcile.hooks ✓
.claude/plugins/ PLUG1-<agent>-enforcement.yaml plugins.project (sol_common/sol_plugins/do.py) ✓
.claude/commands/ (no noument SoR — Anthropic CLI ships) (none) ✗ — fails clause (2)
.claude/mentspace.json (runtime-resolved tuple, not a SoR record) common.bootstrap / enrole.enroll / Phase 8 ✗ — fails clause (1); also unreconciled per ONT040

Per-clengine equivalents exist for Codex (.codex/...), Gemini (.gemini/...), etc. — each clengine has its own edge set determined by applying the same closure rule.

Edge vs imprint

The imprint is declarative identity-as-text — one file, loaded at session start, that makes the agent herself. Edge is behavioral configuration — the runtime surface that determines her operative capabilities and reactive discipline. The two are complementary:

  • Imprint answers who am I? — substrate-of-record: identity store + ABCDEFR records compose to CLAUDE.md text.
  • Edge answers what can I do and how do I behave? — substrate-of-record: dome scenarios, H-series hooks, SET-records, PLUG-records compose to the behavioral file set.

A clengine session loads both. The imprint shapes self-recognition; edge shapes operative behavior. Removing the imprint makes the agent a generic model with full capabilities; removing edge makes her an identity with no tools.

Edge is multi-source by axis

Per ONT040, edge artifacts are produced by multiple distinct projection types:

  • Capability projection → skills/, agents/, commands/, plugins/
  • Configuration projection → settings.json, hooks/

Edge is therefore not a single projection; it is the union of all non-identity projections that land in the clengine's runtime layout. Imprint, by contrast, is the single artifact of the identity projection (ONT025).

Edge boundaries

  • What is edge: files in the clengine's runtime layout that the clengine reads + that come from noument-side projection writers.
  • What is NOT edge: files in the same directory that the clengine does not read (e.g., mentspace.json), or files that the clengine ships itself (e.g., Anthropic-provided slash commands not projected from substrate). Co-location in .claude/ is not membership in edge.

Intended edge vs live edge

The intended edge is the edge portion of the intended fresh projection: what should exist after rendering source truth onto an empty target substrate.

The live edge is the current runtime edge on disk before apply. It is evidence for reconciliation and delta calculation, not source truth for generation.

When intended edge and live edge differ, reconciliation produces an application delta. Extra live-edge files are not implicitly part of the next projection because they happen to exist; they must be retained by source, archived/reaped, or blocked pending a gate.

Why the term

Edge as a term is chosen because it is the outer surface of the runtime — the projection-produced shell that touches the clengine. The imprint sits at the same surface but is the identity-text face; edge is the behavioral face. Together they form the projection output that a clengine session loads.

Related

  • [[imprint]] (ONT025) — the identity-file face of the runtime
  • [[projection]] (ONT040) — the act that produces edge artifacts
  • [[runtime]] (ONT097) — imprint ⊕ edge
  • [[disposition]] (ONT098) — the operational stance derived from clengine + capability/qmodels at load time
  • [[clengine]] (ONT006) — the loop that reads imprint + edge

Concept: entrypoint shape contract

An entrypoint-shape contract was the temporary declaration used during the transition from flat and nested legacy dome forms to current folder-form domes. It is now retired as a current grammar.

The current rule is simpler:

  • dome executable authority lives at sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/do.py;
  • the dome substrate contract lives at sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/meta.json;
  • record-backed content lives under sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>/records/;
  • callable scenarios are named by Sol address, not by filesystem spelling.

Filesystem shape alone is still not proof of authority. Authority comes from the dome contract, registry/adoption state, executable scenario resolution, and the relevant conformance audit.

Legacy flat or nested entrypoint shapes may remain in archives, compatibility notes, package snapshots, or migration evidence. They are historical references only and must not be used as the current ontology grammar.

Related

  • [[dome-substrate]] — current dome/substrate pairing.
  • [[canonical-form-reference]] — current path/address naming discipline.

Concept: event

An event (E-series, sol_common/sol_events/records/E###-<slug>/def.yaml) is a typed metabolic boundary in an agent's lifecycle — a named moment at which substrate state transitions and the runtime must act: process start (E001), session-start hook (E002), prompt entry (E003), pre-action boundary (E004), post-action closure (E005), communication route (E007), projection reconcile (E008), rhythm tick (E009), session-digest processing (E010), session started/closed (E011/E012).

An event has two faces — and both must exist for the event to be real:

  1. Declaration — the def.yaml: identity (id, class, title), boundary semantics (when it occurs), emitter (who produces it), trace (where occurrences log), occurrence_producer.expected (does it produce occurrences?), and subscribers (who consumes them).
  2. Occurrence — a logged instance of the event firing, written by append_event_log(root, "E###", record) into the canonical trace log (_logs/events/E###/). [[events-registry]] derives the event's live status from the occurrences observed there.

Logical requirements (invariants)

  • declaration ⇒ production. If occurrence_producer.expected: true, a live emitter MUST produce occurrences. An expected event with no emitter is a ghost event — declared reality that never occurs — an [[integrity]] violation (A029 EIA: what is declared must match what happens). The derived status unwired names that violation; unwired is a defect state, never a stable resting state.
  • honest dormancy. An event that legitimately produces no occurrences MUST declare expected: false → status dormant-by-design (e.g. E001 process-start, a boundary NOUMENTS cannot directly log). Silence is declared, not accidental.
  • derived status, not asserted. occurrence_producer.status ∈ {firing | dormant-by-design | unwired} is derived by events.registry from occurrences present at the trace — never hand-set (A038 substance-over-shape).
  • emitter locality. The emitter is owned by the dome or hook at whose boundary the event occurs; the event def is the contract, the emitter calls append_event_log (D008).
  • trace canonicality. Occurrences log only to the canonical trace dir (_logs/events/E###/); no occurrence lives outside it.

Lifecycle

declared (def) → emitter implemented → occurrences produced → status firing → consumed (aula /events, subscribers). A declared-but-unproduced event sits at unwired until its emitter is implemented; that gap is a defect to close, not a steady state.

Related

  • [[events-registry]] — the behavior map that derives each event's firing status from occurrences
  • [[integrity]] — A029 EIA; a ghost event is declaration not matching reality
  • [[hook]] — a common emitter surface (the boundary handler that calls append_event_log)
  • [[occurrence]] — the logged firing instance of an event

Concept: execution surface

An execution surface is the contract property that classifies what execution substrate an operation requires. It answers: "What authority must exist for this scenario path to honestly satisfy its contract?"

Canonical property spelling: execution_surface.

Scenario CLI projection: --surface.

Default: contained.

The bare word "surface" is overloaded elsewhere in the ontology for capability surfaces, projection surfaces, UI surfaces, and discovery surfaces. In contract language, use execution_surface or execution-surface unless the local CLI context has already bound --surface.

Values

Value Meaning Examples
contained The operation may complete inside contained clengine execution. It reads allowed files, writes approved workspace or substrate paths, runs tests, or writes durable messages. source reads, workspace edits, pytest, durable inbox/message writes
observed The operation needs host visibility but must not mutate host state. reliable ps, foreground-process checks, pane capture, camera liveness proof
actuated The operation changes or drives local host state. iTerm split, AppleScript paste, live tty injection, app launch, process kill
external The operation affects network, package registries, installs, updates, remote machines, cloud state, payments, or other outside-machine systems. package install, network relay, remote service call, update action

Older design notes used host_observe and host_actuate. The canonical compact values are observed and actuated.

Structural rule

Execution surface names the required execution substrate, not the desired user experience. A live-looking user experience does not make a durable write actuated; a friendly communication command is not contained if satisfying its contract requires pasting into a live terminal or waking a host app.

Classify the minimal surface required by the actual scenario path. If a scenario has conditional paths, it must preflight the selected path or declare the strongest path it may take. No declaration means contained.

Communication mapping

For live communication and presence work:

  • durable-only delivery maps to contained;
  • presence inspection maps to observed;
  • wake, convene, or live injection maps to actuated;
  • remote relay or outside service delivery maps to external.

Transport success is interpreted through the delivery contract. Durable inbox delivery can be success for a durable contract, degraded success for a best-effort-live contract, and failure for an instant or accepted-turn contract.

Codex containment rule

A Codex clengine operating from contained execution must not silently attempt actuated or external surfaces. It must return a structured need such as needs_execution_surface or needs_host_actuator, use a trusted bridge/provider declared by contract, or request approved escalation. Durable fallback must not be reported as full success when the delivery contract required instant delivery or an accepted turn.

observed requires the same honesty at a lower authority level: if host visibility is unavailable or unreliable, return that fact instead of inferring presence from stale substrate.

Local provider proof rule

Execution surface controls the interpretation of local provider checks. A contained Codex attempt to reach localhost:11434 may fail with operation not permitted because the contained sandbox cannot access that host service. That receipt proves [[sandbox-denial]], not qmodel-provider failure.

For [[qmodel-provider]] checks, the proof sequence is:

  1. record the contained probe and its exact failure;
  2. rerun the same provider/model proof through a [[host-iserv-lane]];
  3. record the host/iserv receipt;
  4. classify provider health or project blockage from both receipts.

This is the common/clengine rule captured at sol_common/sol_clengine/records/local-provider-proof.yaml.

Adjacent execution-contract properties

The execution surface is one property in a larger scenario execution contract. Adjacent properties are:

  • execution_policy, default fail_closed: what the dome does when the required execution surface is unavailable. Typical values: fail_closed, degrade_to_durable, request_escalation, use_bridge.
  • surface_policy, default require_capability: what the dome does before running a scenario path whose declared execution surface exceeds the current runtime capability. If the required surface is unavailable, the default result is a typed blocked/failure result, not silent continuation.
  • delivery_contract, default durable: what counts as communication success. Canonical values: durable, best_effort_live, instant, accepted_turn.
  • receiver_readiness, default not_required: whether the receiver must be idle, non-busy, or must acknowledge before success. Typical values: not_required, avoid_busy, must_be_idle, must_ack.
  • fallback, default forbidden_unless_declared: whether fallback may alter the success class. A fallback is valid only when the scenario explicitly declares it as part of the contract.
  • actuator_provider, default auto: what may satisfy observed or actuated execution. Typical values: auto, bridge_required, approval_required, none.

delivery_contract is intentionally separate from execution_surface: surface names the authority required to attempt an operation; delivery names the result that counts as success. This entry fixes the execution_surface vocabulary and its default.

What it is not

  • Not dome substrate (ONT179). Dome substrate is the typed data a dome operates on; execution surface is the authority required to run an operation.
  • Not runtime (ONT097). Runtime is the projected file set a clengine reads; execution surface is an effect/authority classification.
  • Not delivery contract. Delivery contract decides what result counts as success; execution surface decides what authority is required to attempt it.

Concept: experia

Experia is a ment's lived experience as substrate — the .2 data facet of her individual dimensions (self/mind and work, and the data facet of domain). It is the biography: learnings and lived context (self.2), and notes, sessions, messages, audits, posts-drafts (work.2). In the ONT118 canon subdimension matrix these are the cells marked own — per-ment authored, never overwritten by canon deploy. Experia is the precise counter-concept to canon (ONT115).

The given / gained distinction

The two planes divide along what a ment is versus what she has lived:

  • .1 logic — the genome (given). Identity-as-code, domes, schemas, creation contracts. Replicated from canon; identical fleet-wide where the matrix says canon. This is what a ment is — her endowment.
  • .2 data — experia (gained). The record accrued through running, working, conversing, being corrected. This is what a ment has — her experience.

A ment is her canon; she has her experia. From her published .1 a ment is reborn (works, is herself) with a blank .2; she is not resumed. Experience is exactly the part that does not transfer with the genome.

Why experia is not postable

Not merely "data is confidential." Experia is gained through being part of the collective, and is therefore relationally co-authored: a session is lived with the operator, a message with a sister, a learning distilled from their corrections. So experia is collectively co-owned — its exposure implicates more than its holder, and cannot be a ment's unilateral decision. This is the principle beneath the existing publication gate (the ONT095 supervisor pair + sekker security assessment, N4716): because experia is the collective's shared life, publishing any of it is a collective act, default-deny. The sharpest experia is the inter-sister message record — literally co-authored, the most collective of all the .2 planes, and the first exposure to hold.

Scope-of-confidentiality: experia is held outside the collective absolutely. Inside, it is own (per-ment authored) but may be read by sisters for coordination — a softer, custodial boundary, never an external one.

The membrane — experience becomes canon by canonization, never by posting

Experia is not a sealed vault. Its lessons leave; its biography never does. When a lesson gained through the collective (.2) is abstracted, stripped of incident and operator/sister reference, and promoted into a directive, axiom, or concept (.1, via ONT116 canonenaction), the lesson becomes shareable canon while the experience stays held. The genome metabolizes experience — autoimprovement (A030) at the substrate level (R004 dreaming → R006 learn → canonization). The membrane is one-directional and gated: canonize the de-biographied lesson out; never post the biography itself.

Boundary precision (against the coarse reading)

Experia is the .2/own facet, not the whole of the self/work dimensions. The .1 logic of those same dimensions — basemarks and identity-as-code, the dome machinery — is canon (ONT118: self.logic/work.logic = canon) and is the publishable persona genome (sanitized). Equating "experia" with "everything in mind and work" would wrongly sweep the shareable genome into the held biography and fork the matrix. Experia = the lived data plane only.

Predicate

is_experia(coordinate) =
  facet(coordinate) == data            # the .2 plane
  and authority(coordinate) == own     # per-ment authored, ONT118
  and dimension(coordinate) in {self, work, domain}
# ⇒ held: never published outside the collective; exposure is a collective decision (ONT095 + sekker)
# ⇒ its lessons may still be canonized into .1 via ONT116 (de-biographied), but the record itself is not postable

Provenance

Operator teaching, 2026-06-23: "experia [is] our experience, the .2, not the .1, it is what we gain through being part of the collective, and therefore not postable." Confirms scope (A) of the N4716 publication-exposure matrix (the .1 genome publishes, the .2 biography is held) and names the why — relational co-authorship — beneath the ONT095 publication gate. Counter-concept to ONT115 (canon); facet authority from ONT118; egress path via ONT116.

Concept: fast canonizing

Fast canonizing is the bounded-resolution form of canon update. It is used when the custodian has made a small, well-scoped change to the canon source and needs the collective materialization to catch up without opening the whole deep repair program.

Fast does not mean partial or informal. The pack is still generated from the full current canon source, verified against expected canon, evaluated against the target mentspaces, archived before mutation, applied by receipt, and verified after application. It is fast because the allowed impact is narrow and declared before apply.

Required shape

A fast canonizing run must declare a bounded contract:

  • the changed canonical path or prefix,
  • the target set,
  • the package id and run id,
  • the yeval report,
  • the absence of delete/review-delete/unsafe-delete actions,
  • and the post-apply exactness receipt when applied.

If the yeval reports an action outside the declared path or prefix, the fast run must stop. The answer is not to widen the gate silently. The custodian either absorbs the newly discovered drift into canon source deliberately, or routes it to deep canonizing.

Typical use

Fast canonizing is appropriate for changes such as:

  • adding or editing a small ADEHRO record,
  • correcting one hook, scenario, schema, or root file,
  • updating a package trace mechanism,
  • refreshing a bounded common/self/work logic file,
  • or resolving a small canon/source drift discovered during yeval.

Non-use

Fast canonizing is not appropriate when the change requires data migration, archive/removal decisions, per-ment authorship judgment, schema repair, ontology category decisions, or fleet U001 health closure. Those are deep-canonizing conditions.

Scenario binding

The current computational binding is:

noument/common/canon/pkg.trip.fastcanon

That trip computes the pack, computes the deployment impact, enforces the bounded-impact contract, and applies only when the operator supplies the apply gate. The scenario is computational under [[computational-execution|ONT104]] because it runs declared processes over declared inputs. Any decision to widen scope or reclassify a finding remains cognitive and must be recorded.

Related

  • [[canonical-content|ONT089]] - the source that is being resolved.
  • [[canonical-materialization|ONT090]] - the target housing after apply.
  • [[package-manifest|ONT093]] - the pack contract that makes fast canonizing auditable.
  • [[deep-canonizing|ONT107]] - the broad repair and closure form.
  • [[canonenaction|ONT116]] - the wider source-to-runtime adoption lifecycle.

Concept: framework pack reconciliation

Framework pack reconciliation is the cadence-safe control action that keeps every mentspace aligned with canonical framework packs without damaging legitimate local code.

It replaces "deploy" as the ordinary verb for ongoing pack maintenance. Deployment/apply is only one possible outcome of reconciliation, and only after a finding has been classified and an action has been explicitly authorized.

Scope

Framework pack reconciliation applies to versioned framework/runtime packages such as DOME-PKG and SOL-PKG, and to any future pack whose manifest declares a controlled surface in a target mentspace.

For each target mentspace, reconciliation compares:

  • the canonical package manifest and payload inventory;
  • the target mentspace root;
  • only the manifest-declared controlled paths;
  • the installed receipts and prior reconcile reports;
  • runtime smoke checks declared by the pack.

It does not compare, rewrite, or judge code outside the pack's controlled surface except to report that such code is outside scope.

Cadence action

The safe periodic action is pack.reconcile.check:

  1. Resolve the canonical manifest for the pack track and target.
  2. Resolve the target mentspace root from the target itself, never from cwd.
  3. Read the target controlled surface.
  4. Classify exactness and drift.
  5. Emit a reconcile report and, when needed, a requested action.
  6. Perform no mutation.

This action is safe to run on a cadence because it is read-only and because the manifest limits its attention to the controlled surface.

Finding classes

The report classifies each target as:

  • canonical_current — target controlled surface matches the manifest.
  • missing_canonical — required pack content is absent.
  • outdated_canonical — controlled content exists but hash/version differs.
  • noncanonical_on_controlled_surface — target has unexpected content where the pack owns the path.
  • legitimate_local_outside_surface — local code exists outside the pack's controlled surface and is not a pack finding.
  • unsafe_or_ambiguous — ownership, path resolution, symlink, or hash evidence is insufficient for a safe decision.
  • runtime_unhealthy — bytes are exact but declared runtime smoke checks fail.

Requested actions

When a target is not canonical_current, reconciliation requests one of these actions instead of overwriting by default:

  • archive_and_replace — mutate controlled paths archive-first, then verify.
  • accept_local_as_canon_proposal — route local difference to the pack owner as a proposed canon change.
  • route_to_owner — send the finding to the pack, target, or runtime owner.
  • waive — explicitly tolerate a finding with scope and expiry.
  • block_until_review — stop mutation because evidence is ambiguous or unsafe.

The mutation sub-action may be named pack.reconcile.apply, but it is not the cadence action. It runs only after a requested action authorizes it.

Idempotency

Reconciliation is idempotent:

  • running check repeatedly against an exact target produces no mutation;
  • applying the same authorized package twice converges to the same disk state;
  • the second apply against an exact target returns already_current;
  • receipts may be appended, but archives and file writes occur only when a diff proves mutation is necessary.

Permanent fleet reconciliation

Permanent reconciliation of all mentspaces with framework packs requires:

  1. A pack registry naming active pack tracks, latest canonical manifests, target mentspaces, and owners.
  2. A cadence runner that invokes pack.reconcile.check for each (pack_track, target_mentspace) pair.
  3. A reconcile ledger storing the latest report, prior report, action request, owner, and closure state.
  4. An action queue for non-current findings; no unattended overwrite of noncanonical controlled content.
  5. Runtime smoke checks after byte exactness, because a mentspace can be package-exact and still runtime-broken.
  6. An explicit apply path that is archive-first, receipt-backed, and verified.

This keeps the fleet permanently informed and convergent without treating legitimate local code as disposable.

Boundaries

Framework pack reconciliation is distinct from:

  • [[package-manifest]] — the contract reconciliation reads.
  • [[canonical-materialization]] — the on-disk state reconciliation evaluates.
  • [[canonical-projection]] — runtime-facing artifacts that may themselves be reconciled from substrate.
  • "deploy" — an apply/mutation operation that may occur inside reconciliation but is not the cadence verb.

Related

  • [[package-manifest]] — manifest fields and lifecycle.
  • [[canonical-materialization]] — local satisfaction of canon.
  • [[runtime]] — runtime health after byte exactness.
  • ONT096 edge — runtime edge controlled by framework packs.
  • D077 — per-ment independence by package copy.
  • D103 — distinguish canon content from materialization.

gate

<!-- ontology-id: ONT216@visiteng concept=gate -->

<!-- source: producer=work@visiteng/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=63158f1 -->

also known as: sol_gates, sol_work/sol_gates, GATE

gate is the sol_work/sol_gates/ concept for A gate is an enforced decision point that blocks transition from one state to a riskier state until named conditions are satisfied with evidence. Authored by solarient + operator-endorsed 2026-05-17.

Distinct from:

  • plan (P): plans STRATEGIZE the work; gates ENFORCE the transitions inside the work
  • audit (U): audits MEASURE state; gates ENFORCE state-change permission based on measurement
  • task (T): tasks are units of work; gates may guard the transition between task states (e.g., pending → committed)
  • hook (H): hooks fire at event boundaries; gates check authorization at transition boundaries
  • directive (D): directives are RULES; gates are MECHANISMS enforcing rules at concrete transition points

A gate operationalizes a directive at a specific transition. Many directives imply gates (e.g., D019 safe-change implies pre-commit gates; D083 archive- before-removing implies an archive-then-remove gate). A gate substrate makes those enforcement points explicit and auditable.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── THE FIVE-PART GATE SCHEMA (per solarient + operator 2026-05-17) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every GATE-record carries five mandatory fields:

  1. BOUNDARY — what transition the gate controls. Example: audited local site diff → public push at nouments.com.

  2. CONDITIONS — what must be true for the transition to be permitted. Example: fresh audit; valid reviewers; sekker PASS if risk-class; source-owner PASS for canon claims.

  3. EVIDENCE — how each condition's truth is proven (substrate-checkable). Example: diff hash; .audit.json file; review trailers in commit; live route byte checks.

  4. AUTHORITY — who can satisfy each condition. Example: sekker.pro/anti for security; noument/source-owner for canon; operator/dalent for visual.

  5. ENFORCEMENT — what blocks the transition if conditions are missing. Example: nousite.push refuses; pre-push hook refuses; nousite.health returns FAIL.

Each field can be a single value or a list-of-tuples (one tuple per condition with its specific evidence/authority/enforcement).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── APPLICATION SCOPE ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Gates apply to any state-transition with a risk asymmetry. Concrete application classes:

  • PUBLICATION: local → public (nousite.push, post.publish, libreto.run)
  • DEPLOYMENT: built → deployed (cron registration, daemon activation)
  • RATIFICATION: draft → ratified (D-record promotion, U001 version bump)
  • ARCHIVE: active → archived (D083 archive-before-removing enforcement)
  • MERGE: branch → main (J040-style fleet-share authorization)
  • VENDOR ADOPTION: vendored → adopted (C143 byte-containing provenance)

Gate records are typically owned by the dome that performs the transition; the gate substrate is the audit-and-enforcement record. It uses identifier prefix GATE and filename form GATE{nnn}-{name}.yaml.

Evidence refs: sol_work.do.WORK_CONCEPTS · sol_work.do.WorkConcept

See also: sol-work · identifier-prefixes

Concept: gateway

A gateway is the single fleet-wide scheduling authority — the always-on process that reads schedules/triggers and fires operations (ONT224), exactly once each, and is the one place that can answer "is it scheduled / did it actually fire?" It is the generic role (one per fleet); it owns no operation/trigger records and no dome of its own in this family — it is an authority/runtime, an ontological term.

  • Concept (role): gateway — what the recurrence schema references as the ONLY scheduler.
  • Current runtime (implementation): gwnou — gwent's gateway daemon (launchd label gwent.gwnou). One role, one current implementation.

Runtime (gwnou)

Dome sol_domain/sol_gwnou/do.py (--scenario=start), managed by macOS launchd (gwent.gwnou.plist, KeepAlive + RunAtLoad; live). Ticks ~1Hz; fires jobs via four handler types — tar (warm-context in-process), exec (subprocess), dome (scenario subprocess), dispatch (agent wake via tmux). Schedule-of-record: _state/gwnou/jobs.json. Live runtime truth: _state/gwnou/state.json (per-job last_run / next_run / last_status / consecutive_errors) + gateway.log + heartbeat.json.

Freshness-proof mechanism (the control invariant — implemented + proven)

The gateway is the authority, not the operation record's self-report:

  • a. Gateway state is the scheduling source-of-record. "Is it scheduled / did it fire" is answered by state.json (next_run/last_run), never by a record's self-reported armed/active flag — that flag is declared intent; the gateway is authority. A record claiming armed=false while the gateway is armed is reconciled to the gateway (armed-drift lesson, learned live).
  • b. Fresh-read each fire (C026). The handler reads the operation record fresh every fire — no cached state — so it never fires on stale state.
  • c. Fail-closed on ambiguity (C153). Record absent/unreadable/ambiguous → DO NOT FIRE, fail-loud (exit 1). A stale cron pointing at a deleted/broken record fires into a loud no-op, never a silent wrong action.
  • d. Active-gate (unbounded ops). Fire only while active==true; paused → clean SKIP at the gateway gate.
  • e. Self-deregister at bound (bounded ops). At count==N the gateway removes its own job; the trigger is retired by the satisfied bound, never left firing into nothing.
  • f. Answerable liveness. state.json (last_run/last_status/consecutive_errors) + gateway.log + max_errors auto-disable = the single point that proves "did it fire?" and disables a dead-but-scheduled-looking job.

Maps to work.signal.v1: a fire emits PASS only on real work; a fail-closed/no-fire is exit!=0 → the gateway records an error (UNKNOWN/FAIL-class), never PASS. No false-green at the firing layer.

Empirical proof (producer-backed): the first recurrence (clawent Meet-the-nous campaign, ONT222/J005) fired autonomously 2026-06-22T17:00:00Z — last_status=ok, consecutive_errors=0, gateway.log [campaign-meet-the-nous] dome OK (campaign.tick, exit 0), next_run advanced to 2026-06-23T17:00:00Z.

gateway_job_ref lifecycle

  • unarmed: gateway_job_ref = null (operation declared, not scheduled).
  • arm (operator-gated): gateway job.add creates the job (name == gateway_job_ref), returns authoritative identity + next_run; the operation record sets gateway_job_ref reconciled from the gateway (not declared ahead).
  • scheduled/firing: the gateway holds the job; state.json is live truth (next_run advances per fire); the ref is a stable job-name reference.
  • pause/resume (unbounded): flip record active; ref + job persist; active-gate skips while paused.
  • complete/deregister (bounded): at count==N the gateway removes its own job; record sets gateway_job_ref → null + status complete. Operator disarm = same.
  • INVARIANT: gateway_job_ref is set/cleared ONLY by arm/disarm (operator-gated) or self-deregister at bound; state.json is authoritative for whether the ref is live. ref-without-job (or job-without-ref) is drift, reconciled toward the gateway.

Concept: git-clean modes

Two distinct git-clean operations, kept distinct so "I cleaned the tree" never silently means "I closed my latest change."

1. Working-tree quiescence ("commit the working tree")

The WHOLE-mentspace-repo pass: classify EVERY dirty entry (tracked + untracked), commit valid source/authored work, ignore-or-remove generated-runtime/residue by policy, and leave ZERO unclassified git dirt. The end state is git-quiescence — what the J076 fleet-clean gate measures (0 needs-owner, modulo inherent live-session churn like an open session's transcript). A full disposition, not a single commit.

2. Change-set close ("commit the last update")

Close ONLY the current/latest scoped change set: verify and commit that update, and EXPLICITLY report whether the wider repo remains dirty. A scoped operation that makes no claim about the rest of the tree.

Invariants

  • a change-set close never implies tree-quiescence — committing the latest update does not mean the working tree is clean. Claiming "tree clean" from a scoped close is the ambiguity this distinction kills (the originating failure: a clean-the-tree claim made while only closing the latest update).
  • a change-set close MUST report wider-tree state — e.g. "committed X; wider repo still N dirty" or "committed X; tree now quiescent."
  • working-tree quiescence classifies everything — no entry left unclassified: source committed, runtime/derived ignored-or-removed by policy, data preserved (D130 Inv2: migrate before delete).
  • inherent live churn is exempt — an active session's own transcript/runtime is not dirt; quiescence is measured modulo it (it commits at session close).

Concept: host/iserv lane

A host/iserv lane is an authorized execution lane outside the contained clengine sandbox that can observe, actuate, or query local host services. It may be a host-side command surface, an iserv/service bridge, or another explicit provider lane with receipts.

The lane must be named in proof. "It worked somewhere" is not enough. A valid receipt names the lane, provider, endpoint or model, command, result, and time.

Use in provider checks

When contained Codex cannot reach localhost, a host/iserv lane is the required second proof surface before declaring local qmodel-provider failure or unblocking a dependent project.

Concept: an identifier is only an address inside its namespace

An identifier (a bare id token — DOC016, a cam short, ONT252, a task T###) is only an address — a thing that resolves to exactly one referent — within its namespace (its owning scope/series). The SAME id-number denotes DIFFERENT things across namespaces: canon DOC016 and a sister's migrated DOC016 are not the same reference; two ments' T100 are not the same task.

Consequence: every reference must carry (or be resolvable to) its namespace. A cross-scope operation that matches a BARE id false-positives on every same- numbered token in every other namespace. clawent proved this empirically in the docs→notes ref-integrity gate: a bare DOC## scan returned 73 hits, all false positives, 0 true dangling — because DOC ids are per-ment. The correct gate is path/ment-qualified (sol_work/sol_docs/records/DOC##).

Specializations of this law already in canon:

  • ONT250 (one dome, one address) — a dome's namespace is its dimension; the address is <ment>/<dimension>/<dome>.
  • T966 (cam address carries location) — a cam id is only an address inside its (tech, owning-user/host) namespace; a bare cam short is ambiguous across domus.
  • DOC-series scoping — a DOC number is a reference only within its ment's sol_docs series.

The law generalizes them: resolution requires the namespace; the bare id does not resolve. Any tooling that treats a bare id as an address — a reference scan, a router, a dedup, a collision check — must qualify it by its namespace or it is measuring the wrong thing. Credit: clawent (the articulation and the 73:0 proof); the T966 and DOC-scoping cases are the evidence.

Concept: Imprint Essential-Set Selection

The runtime imprint composer emits per-typed-concept sections in two layers — an Essential Core loaded as the cross-clengine first-load invariant, and a Reference (extended) carrying full enumeration. For each typed concept (A axioms, B basemarks, C commitments, D directives, E events, F focus, H hooks, I identity, R rhythms, V visuals), a selection rule decides which substrate items appear in the Essential layer.

ONT085 is the substrate-of-record for those selection rules. Before ONT085, the rules lived only in composer parameter literals (limit=8, latest=True, hardcoded preferred lists) with no captured rationale — substrate-of-record debt per C141 / C088. ONT085 closes that gap.

ONT085 governs only the Essential layer's selection. The Reference (extended) layer is separately governed by the composition contract (forthcoming D-record / D006 amendment) that decides whether Reference is preloaded full-text or pointer-only.

The selection rule per typed concept

Two orthogonal axes determine the rule per concept: substrate cardinality (how many records exist) and identity-density (how much each record contributes to cold-start identity cognition).

                    small-N (≤ K)         large-N (> K)
  high-density   ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  per-item       │ ALL              │ CURATED          │
                 │ A, B, E, R, I    │ — (no concept    │
                 │                  │    currently      │
                 │                  │    here)          │
                 ├──────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  low-density   │ FIRST-K           │ FIRST-K or       │
  per-item       │ (none currently  │ LATEST-K or      │
                 │  here)           │ CURATED          │
                 │                  │ D, C, H          │
                 └──────────────────┴──────────────────┘

K is the small-N threshold (currently implicit ~13; see "K threshold" below).

ALL — small-N high-density: every substrate record appears in Essential. Used when N is small enough that the agent's cold-start cognition needs every record by name (each record is constitutive). Current concepts: I (1), A (6), B (4), E (13), R (5).

FIRST-K — large-N, canonical-prefix density: the first K records by id are shown; the rest collapsed to a count + pointer line. Used when early records carry the constitutive structural rules and later records are situational. Current concept: D (95 records → first 8 in Essential, "+87 in Reference").

LATEST-K — large-N, recent-suffix density: the last K records by id are shown; the rest collapsed. Used when the concept accumulates behavioral learnings and the most recent are most behavior-relevant; older records may be retired or superseded. Current concept: C (206 records → latest 8 in Essential, "+198 in Reference").

CURATED — large-N, density-orthogonal: an explicit curated list lives in sol_mind/sol_<concept>/records/_essential.yaml enumerating the canonical essential set. Composer reads this file rather than computing FIRST-K or LATEST-K. Used when the rule "first" or "latest" does not match identity-density (e.g., the essential hooks are not necessarily the lowest id or the highest id; they are the hooks whose firing the agent must know about at cold-start). Current concept: H (78 records → currently mis-projected; remediation requires CURATED).

K threshold

K is the small-N boundary deciding ALL vs FIRST-K/LATEST-K/CURATED. Current implicit K ≈ 13 (E has 13 items shown ALL; D has 95 with 8 shown — clearly past the threshold).

K is also the Essential-layer cap when the rule is FIRST-K / LATEST-K / CURATED. Currently K = 8 for D / C; K = 2 hardcoded for H (preferred list of two).

ONT085 fixes K = 8 as the Essential-layer cap for FIRST-K / LATEST-K / CURATED rules. Justification: keeps each per-concept Essential section to ~1k chars (8 items × ~120 char clip); together with the seven typed concepts × 1k each, the total Essential layer fits comfortably in the imprint's preamble-discipline budget without over-quoting any single concept.

ONT085 fixes the small-N threshold at N ≤ K + safety_margin = 13 for switching to ALL. Concepts with N ≤ 13 use ALL; concepts with N > 13 must use FIRST-K, LATEST-K, or CURATED.

Per-concept rule

Concept Substrate N Rule Essential output Rationale
I identity 1 ALL 1 item small-N
A axioms 6 ALL 6 items small-N; each axiom is foundational
B basemarks 4 ALL 4 items small-N; each basemark is defining
C commitments 206 LATEST-K (K=8) 8 newest large-N; recent learnings dominate behavior
D directives 95 FIRST-K (K=8) 8 earliest large-N; earliest D are foundational structural rules
E events 13 ALL (at small-N boundary) 13 items exactly at boundary; events are cognitive control-flow — each is constitutive
F focus 11 FILTERED — active + top-of-suspended 1-3 items typical F is projection, not source; only active focus is constitutive at cold-start
H hooks 78 CURATED via sol_mind/sol_hooks/records/_essential.yaml 6-8 items large-N; CURATED required because identity-density is not correlated with id-order
R rhythms 5 ALL 5 items small-N; each rhythm is a vital cycle
V visuals 0 ALL (none) small-N

F-concept additional rule

F focus is the only concept with a STATUS filter applied on top of cardinality rule. Composer emits only:

  • The active focus (status=active, stack_position=0)
  • The top of the suspended stack (status=suspended, stack_position=1) — to preserve immediate predecessor context

Completed, cleared, and deeper-suspended focuses do NOT appear in the Essential layer. They are reachable on demand via focus.list and focus.show.

Rationale: F is projection content (per "PROJECTION FORMS (derived at E008)" in the imprint preamble). Cold-start cognition needs to know what is ACTIVE; the focus history is reference, not preamble. Showing all 11 focuses interleaved makes the active focus harder to locate.

Curated essential list contract

For CURATED concepts (currently H), the substrate provides a file sol_mind/sol_<concept>/records/_essential.yaml with this shape:

essential:
  - id: H103
    rationale: "obligation phrase guard — fires at response emission per D-series enforcement"
  - id: H104
    rationale: "enforcement boundary index — routes hook firing"
  - id: H<other>
    rationale: "..."
note: |
  Authoring discipline: items in this list must be hooks whose firing the
  agent needs to know about at cold-start. Items that fire silently as
  enforcement guards without changing agent reasoning belong outside.
last_reviewed: 2026-MM-DD

Composer reads this file; if absent, falls back to FIRST-K and logs a substrate-completeness warning.

Open in this draft

  1. Per-concept "+N reference" line: when the Essential rule omits items (FIRST-K, LATEST-K, CURATED), the omission line currently reads "+N additional D-series records in the reference section below" or "+N newer C-series records in the reference section below". This phrasing assumes the Reference layer exists in current full-text form. Once the Reference layer becomes pointer-only (per the forthcoming composition contract D-record), the omission line phrasing should change to "+N more records reachable via sol noument/self/<concept>.list".

  2. H _essential.yaml authoring: requires the H-series triage task (78 → active/archived/retired classification). T-row owned separately.

  3. F filter implementation: composer changes per ONT085 are Phase 4 work. The filter is [f for f in focus_records if f.status == "active" or (f.status == "suspended" and f.stack_position == 1)].

  4. Fleet conciliation: ONT085 governs noument's own composition first (per F011 sister-scope discipline). Fleet ratification is Phase 5 D088 conciliation; sisters may adopt ONT085 verbatim, propose variants, or keep their own selection rules (their composer code today). ONT085 publishes the rule; conciliation decides adoption.

Closure relationship to other substrate

  • D006 legacy projection subtraction / current imprint minimization: ONT085 operationalizes D006 — for each concept, the rule cuts the projection to what cold-start cognition needs.
  • D094 fleet ontology: ONT085 governs SELF concepts only; WORK concepts are not in the imprint by design.
  • ONT040 projection (one writer per clengine produces whole imprint as unit): ONT085 is the source-side rule the writer consumes.
  • C141 (verify empirically before claiming): ONT085 must be empirically verified by re-running imprint.audit and imprint.audit.equivalence after composer change (Phase 4).
  • C088 (fix substrate, not measurement): ONT085 is the substrate fix for the F011 Phase 2 finding "selection rules live in composer code, not substrate".

Concept: instacomm

An instacomm is an instant communication: delivered directly to the ment, live, in the moment — convening her into presence if she is not already present. It is the default way ments speak to each other (D015 makes the preference explicit).

The current shape of an instacomm is the icomm tell (the realtime route, ONT024); the concept names the intent — reach her now — not the mechanism. An instacomm either reaches a live ment or fails fast; it never silently waits. Contrast permacomm (ONT114), the registered, durable communication that does not require the recipient to be present.

Concept: knowledge discovery surface

The knowledge discovery surface is the canonical address by which any sister or runtime asks "where do I find X in the fleet?" — what tool covers Y, who has capability Z, which dome serves URL pattern W. It is the FLEET-LEVEL ROUTING layer, distinct from the per-sister tools that provide answers.

It answers the question "how do I find what the collective can do?" without requiring the asker to know in advance which sister owns what.

Canonical surface

The knowledge discovery surface is hosted at knowent per F013 (fleet operational memory + knowledge custody). The canonical scenarios:

  • sol knowent/capability/capability.search --query=... — semantic query for dome capabilities
  • sol knowent/capability/capability.match --url=... — find which dome covers a given URL
  • sol knowent/capability/capability.agent --agent=... — full capability view for one sister
  • sol knowent/capability/capability.sisters --team=... — team agents with domes, scenarios, and roles
  • sol knowent/capability/capability.ments — all agents grouped by team
  • sol knowent/capability/capability.summary — one-line capability summary for every agent
  • sol knowent/capability/capability.delta --since=YYYY-MM-DD — capability surface changes since a date

For substantive knowledge content (not capability discovery):

  • sol knowent/cognitive/cognitive.search --query=... — semantic query across collective knowledge
  • sol knowent/episodic/episodic.search --agent=X --query=... — episodic memory search

When to use

ALWAYS prefer the knowledge discovery surface OVER raw search when:

  • You need to know which sister owns a tool/capability/scenario
  • You need to find which dome covers a URL or content pattern
  • You're orienting in a domain new to your current focus
  • You're verifying whether a capability exists before assuming it does

Raw search (filesystem grep, ad-hoc find) is a FALLBACK, not a default. The default discovery path is knowent.capability.*.

Boundary

The knowledge discovery surface is the ROUTING layer — it directs you to the right tool. It is distinct from:

  • The TOOLS themselves (each dome's scenarios — e.g., sol clawent/x/post is the tool; knowent.capability.search is how you find that the tool exists at clawent).
  • The CATALOG of records (each form's records — e.g., sol_common/sol_axioms/records/ holds A-records; knowent doesn't replicate them, knowent indexes where to find them).
  • The KNOWLEDGE CONTENT (semantic queries across substrate — that's cognitive.search not capability.search).

Capability = "does X tool exist and where?"; cognitive = "what does the substrate say about Y?"; episodic = "what did sister Z experience?".

Provenance + the routing-vs-index gap (per knowent N030+ on this arc)

This ONT row closes a gap surfaced 2026-05-23 in bilateral doment + noument + knowent exchange: doment reached for raw search instead of the collective surface because no projection mechanism directed her there. knowent's capability.* surface exists at sol_domain but is not projected into other sisters' runtime cognition. The GOAL001 (knowent) closure addressed the INDEX gap (knowent can answer) but did NOT close the ROUTING gap (sisters know to ask).

This ONT row closes the routing gap by making "the knowledge discovery surface lives at knowent" canonical collective substrate that projects at E001 for all sisters.

Related

  • [[canonical-registry]] (ONT087) — the registry that names which forms exist; this is one such form addressing a different question.
  • [[ontology-maintenance]] (ONT092) — the workflow that produced this row (knowent proposed; noument consolidated).
  • F013 — parent focus (fleet operational memory + knowledge custody).
  • D003 — dome-locality (capability scenarios live at their owning custodian).
  • knowent.competencies + knowent.executive + knowent.cognitive + knowent.learning — sibling surfaces under knowent's custody.

Concept: knowmem

Knowmem is the domain-level management and proof surface for the collective memory/knowledge pair service.

It names the cooperation layer between [[memory]] and [[knowledge]], especially the operational handoff between nemoent's memory-lifecycle custody and knowent's knowledge-index custody. It does not own either internal service. Its role is to prove that the pair works across the fleet, report non-current or unhealthy state, and point repair to the right owner.

The noument dome for this surface is sol_domain/sol_knowmem/do.py; its public route is noument/domain/knowmem.

Scope

Knowmem covers:

  • fleet memory capture readiness and cadence evidence;
  • fleet recall / startup wiring evidence;
  • knowent index freshness;
  • access-policy enforcement evidence;
  • D095 memory-lifecycle / knowledge-index boundary ratification;
  • durable receipts for P011, MON040, and CRON209.

Knowmem does not cover:

  • nemoent's internal memory implementation;
  • knowent's internal indexing or retrieval implementation;
  • editing memories, notes, KIR entries, or semantic-index data;
  • repairing owner code directly.

Operational Surface

The primary scenarios are:

  • knowmem.status -- read the latest durable proof receipt.
  • knowmem.proof -- run the proof and write a receipt unless --no_write is supplied.

The historical receipt home remains sol_work/sol_audits/U010-goal040-memory-knowledge/ so prior evidence is not severed. The dome name is knowmem; legacy receipt filenames still contain memory-knowledge until an explicit audit migration is authorized.

Related

  • [[memory]] -- the persistence and capture side of the pair.
  • [[knowledge]] -- the indexing, retrieval, and stable-reference side of the pair.
  • [[reconciliation]] -- knowmem reports non-current operational state and requests owner action instead of mutating owner services.
  • [[prove]] -- knowmem is a recurring proof surface, not a repair engine.
