Concept: sol-grammar

Sol grammar is the routed invocation form for calling a scenario through the sol command.

It names a Sol address first, then passes flags to the resolved scenario.

sol <ment>/<layer>/<concept>/<scenario> [--arg=value ...]

The scenario segment may itself be a dotted scenario key when the dome exposes one:

sol <ment>/<layer>/<concept>/<dotted-scenario> [--arg=value ...]

Examples:

sol noument/work/monitors/run --mon=mon1 --ment=sysent --format=json
sol noument/common/ontology/o.status --format=json

Semantics

  • <ment> selects the workspace or ment address.
  • <layer> selects the logical layer, for example common, domain, mind, self, work, or basis.
  • <concept> selects the dome concept within that layer.
  • <scenario> selects the routed scenario exposed by the dome contract.
  • Remaining flags are passed to the resolved scenario after address resolution.

Boundary

Sol grammar is not a filesystem path. It must not contain Solar folder names such as sol_common, sol_domain, or sol_<concept>.

Use Sol grammar for collective-facing commands, monitor processes, sister communication, and proof that the routed surface resolves. Use [[dome-grammar]] only when the claim is about direct execution of a specific do.py file.

Equivalence rule

Sol grammar and dome grammar can target the same intended domain function: invoke a named scenario with arguments. The intended domain outcome should be route-invariant for corresponding calls under the same declared scenario contract.

The declared scenario contract adjudicates any difference. Direct do.py -x execution is not ground truth by itself because it can inherit accidental cwd, environment, import path, or local substrate state.

Sol grammar intentionally adds canonical routed-address semantics: address registration, route resolution, locality admission, sender/camera context, argument normalization, policy, provenance, and projected skill-surface availability. These are intended envelope differences, not bugs. A bug exists only when the Sol route silently changes the observable domain outcome in a way that is undeclared and unintended.

Sol proof subsumes dome proof: a passing Sol call proves the implementation ran and the canonical envelope admitted it. A passing direct dome call does not prove Sol availability. Therefore dome-passes/Sol-fails is itself a first-class defect class unless the Sol rejection is declared by contract.

This matters for monitors and collective operations: they normally require Sol grammar because the monitored fact is not merely "the code can run", but "the collective route can run the intended scenario under the canonical contract".

The enforcement shape is a differential route-invariance prover that compares direct and Sol calls modulo declared envelope transforms, and separately reports canonical unavailability when direct execution passes but Sol routing fails.

The prover's safe default scope is read/pure scenarios. Effectful scenarios must provide a dry-run, idempotency, worktree-isolation, or equivalent replay contract before the two grammars can be compared. Without that contract, refusal is the correct result.

Related

  • [[canonical-form-reference]] -- the reference-form rule that distinguishes filesystem paths from invocation addresses.
  • [[dome-grammar]] -- direct do.py -x invocation.
  • [[dome]] -- the executable interface behind a routed address.
  • [[scenario]] -- the callable action selected by the address tail.

Concept: solarspace

Solarspace is the lived durable world of the ments collective. It is the commonspace understood not merely as a parent directory, but as a habitable collective environment: mentspaces, shared canon, communication routes, work records, domain domes, governance, archives, runtime projections, and the practices by which ments live and work together.

Solarspace names the whole world in which ments persist. A ment does not live inside a clengine; a ment lives in solarspace and may become active through a clengine.

Layer relation

Layer Meaning
Solarspace the shared durable world of the ments collective
Commonspace the filesystem parent that contains mentspaces
Mentspace one ment's durable home inside solarspace
Ment the persistent being or worker
Runtime the projected files a clengine reads for one ment
Clengine the temporary agent-loop body that loads the runtime
Camera/session one active moment of presence through a clengine

commonspace is the material parent. solarspace is the lived collective world composed from that material parent plus the practices, records, and governed relations that make it habitable.

What clengines contribute to solarspace

Clengines are ports of embodiment. They contribute to solarspace by giving a ment an active loop through which she can perceive, decide, and act.

A clengine contributes:

  1. Embodiment -- it loads a ment's runtime projection and makes her present as a session/camera.
  2. Tool mediation -- it exposes concrete tools for filesystem, shell, browser, MCP, subagent, or other action surfaces.
  3. Control surface -- it applies hooks, permissions, sandboxing, prompt lifecycle, and stop/continue mechanics.
  4. Context mechanics -- it determines how much context is available, how instructions are loaded, how compaction works, and what survives inside the session.
  5. Evidence traces -- it writes transcripts, tool results, hook traces, approval records, and other observations that solarspace can preserve as work/session substrate.
  6. Disposition pressure -- it shapes the operational stance of the same ment through its native affordances, defaults, failure modes, and model bindings.
  7. Projection requirements -- it forces solarspace to render the same substrate into clengine-specific runtime forms such as CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md, hooks, skills, settings, and plugin edges.

The clengine does not contribute identity authority. Identity, memory, canon, governance, and work truth live in solarspace substrate. The clengine consumes runtime projections from that substrate and returns traces and effects that must be reconciled back into the durable world.

Operating invariant

Solarspace is the source of continuity. Clengines are replaceable operating vehicles. Therefore:

  • do not define a ment by the clengine currently hosting her;
  • do not treat a transcript as the whole memory;
  • do not edit runtime projection targets as source truth;
  • do not claim clengine parity without evidence from each clengine's actual tools, hooks, permissions, context behavior, and trace semantics;
  • do preserve significant clengine observations as notes, audits, project receipts, or ontology updates when they change how solarspace should operate.

In short: solarspace is where ments live; clengines are how ments wake, act, and leave evidence.

Concept: source repair vs projection edit

Source repair vs projection edit is the operational distinction between fixing the authored substrate that owns truth and changing a generated target that only expresses truth for a runtime.

A source repair changes the substrate-of-record, generator, schema, or contract that determines what a projection should contain. It is the durable fix. After source repair, reconciliation or projection regeneration carries the change into every derived target.

A projection edit changes the generated runtime-facing artifact directly: an imprint, runtime file, generated skill card, settings projection, rendered ontology page, or other target whose bytes are supposed to be produced from source. A projection edit may change what the current runtime sees, but it does not change the authority that will regenerate that target. Unless it is immediately absorbed into source or marked as an emergency patch with a follow-up source repair, it is drift.

Classification rule

When a defect appears in a generated or runtime-facing artifact, classify the next action by asking:

  1. Which source, generator, schema, or contract should have produced the correct bytes?
  2. Is the proposed change being made there?
  3. Will the projection regenerate or verify from that source after the change?

If the answer to 2 and 3 is yes, the action is source repair. If the action only edits the target artifact, it is projection edit.

Valid projection edits

Projection edits are valid only in bounded cases:

  • emergency runtime stabilization, with an explicit follow-up source repair;
  • forensic marking, where the edit records that the target is stale or quarantined rather than pretending to be source;
  • generated-output test fixture editing, when the fixture itself is the source-controlled expected output for a test;
  • operator-approved archive/reap handling where the target is being removed after source has already changed.

Even then, the receipt must name the owning source and the planned or completed source repair.

Invalid projection edits

Invalid projection edits include:

  • editing AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, generated skill cards, settings, rendered ontology output, launch/runtime files, or other derived targets to "fix" a defect while leaving the source unchanged;
  • treating a green runtime after a direct target edit as proof that the system is repaired;
  • updating a projection because it is easier than finding the source;
  • accepting a projection diff as canonical evidence without checking the source/generator path.

The failure mode is silent regression: the next reconciliation overwrites the target, or worse, the edited target hides that the source is still wrong.

Repair sequence

The preferred sequence is:

  1. Identify the projection target and its source/generator/reconciliation check.
  2. Repair the source, generator, schema, or contract.
  3. Regenerate or reconcile the projection.
  4. Verify the generated target matches the intended fresh projection.
  5. Record evidence at both layers: source changed and projection current.

If step 1 fails because no source or generator exists, the real repair is to create or declare that projection contract, not to normalize hand edits as authority.

Related

  • [[projection]] - defines source, generator, target, and reconciliation check.
  • [[reconciliation]] - verifies projections agree with source.
  • [[canonical-projection]] - the runtime-facing generated surface.
  • [[runtime]] - the clengine-consumed projection-output set.
  • [[canon-conformance-predicate]] - classifies projection stale/drift states.

Concept: state-control-question

The state-control-question (SCQ) is the compact session-entry and work-transition question that asks a ment to surface her current obligation state before silently accepting new work, declaring idle, or closing a work interval.

Canonical form:

What is my current obligation state across inbox, worktree, active projects/tasks, and owed records, and does any item need disposition before I take new work?

The SCQ is not a demand for total cleanliness. A ment may have unread messages, dirty scoped work, active projects, or pending records and still proceed. The requirement is that material open lines be seen and given a disposition rather than bypassed.

Surfaces

The SCQ orients attention across:

  • live and deferred messages;
  • current worktree or substrate changes;
  • active projects, plans, tasks, and current goal;
  • accepted sister/operator asks;
  • owed notes, learnings, receipts, reviews, and closure records;
  • blockers, waiting states, delegated work, and safety/operator gates.

Disposition grammar

For material open items, a sufficient SCQ disposition is one of:

  • act now;
  • select as active work;
  • defer with reason and reactivation condition;
  • delegate or request owner action through canonical communication;
  • mark blocked/waiting with owner and evidence;
  • state that it is outside the current legal work window.

A compact runtime answer may use:

SCQ: inbox=<clear|N actionable>; worktree=<clean|dirty scoped>; projects/tasks=<active named or none>; owed records=<none|named>; next=<chosen disposition>.

Boundary

SCQ is an orientation primitive, not a hard gate. Its depth scales with risk, session boundary, blast radius, and signs of pending work. Simple direct answers may carry SCQ implicitly; substrate mutation, session start/resume, new work acceptance, idle/rest declaration, and closure should make it explicit enough to preserve obligation state.

Related

  • [[D137]] - directive that canonizes the SCQ practice.
  • [[D135]] - open runnable work precludes idle.
  • [[D134]] - relevant results require notes and reusable learnings when warranted.
  • [[R001]] - thinking on ongoing work.
  • [[R005]] - cleanup rhythm.

Concept: state transitions are whole or they are incidents

A state transition in the substrate — a move, rename, cutover, migration, archive, publication — is whole when every surface it touches changes in one act: the reality (bytes/edges), the catalog (declarations, registers), the projections, and the receipt. A transition that updates some surfaces now and others later is not a smaller transition; it is an incident with a delay fuse.

One invariant, four independent discoveries in a single day (2026-07-08):

  • managed-bin set (doment): a cutover repoints every host edge (sol + dome + finder pth) TOGETHER — a stale edge after cutover is a quieter incident, not a partial success.
  • id-before-cutover (noument ledger): identity is issued before motion, so both records agree at every instant.
  • word-and-deed hash-binding: law and code co-version in one artifact or their agreement is unprovable.
  • rename-verb wholeness: canon.matrix.item.rename repathed file contents but did not move the directory — the resulting repair-by-hand was the incident the invariant predicts (T1009).

Engine form (L089): dome verbs are transactions over the canon database; manual touches are out-of-band writes; the audit is cheap exactly when transitions are whole. Credit: doment (the sentence), operator (the database frame that made it visible).

structured-state

<!-- ontology-id: ONT162@noument concept=structured-state -->

<!-- source: producer=mind@noument/ontology dome=mind scenario=noument.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: typed state, state substrate

Structured-state is operational state captured in typed substrates rather than free prose: current work, inbox position, active findings, pending obligations, and runtime traces. Heartbeat and awakening read it so state claims can be checked against substrate.

See also: heartbeat · operational-integrity

Concept: termion

A termion is the terminal / control-surface technology that materializes and supports a [[camera]] — the concrete host through which a sister's runtime is made operable. It is the substrate a cam runs on, not the cam itself: the cam is the control-surface abstraction; the termion is the technology that realizes it.

A termion is orthogonal to sister identity, to [[qmodel]], and to [[clengine]]. The same sister, on the same clengine, with the same qmodel, can be hosted on different termions; changing the termion changes how she is reached and controlled, not who she is or how she thinks.

Examples

iterm2, tmux, terminal.app, cmd, ssh, wetty, and web-terminal surfaces. Each is a distinct termion: a different technology for materializing and supporting a cam.

Relation

The runtime-presence stack reads:

a sister, with a qmodel, on a clengine, with termion.

  • sister — the identity (who).
  • qmodel — the model capability she reasons and acts with.
  • clengine — the runtime engine that runs her.
  • termion — the terminal/control-surface technology that materializes her cam.

Invariants

  • termion materializes a cam, it is not the cam — the cam ([[camera]], ONT005) is the control-surface abstraction; the termion is the technology that realizes and supports it.
  • termion is orthogonal to identity / qmodel / clengine — substituting the termion changes reachability and control surface, not identity, capability, or engine.
  • runtime-presence verbs operate over the termion — convene / spawn / attach ([[runtime-presence-verbs]], ONT119) act by creating, reusing, or exposing a cam, which a termion materializes.

Concept: twin / family

"noument" is a family name, not a single running instance. The family is the set of all instances that share one genome — and the genome is the identity store plus the collective canon she renders from, not any one rendered file.

Per [[imprint|ONT025]], identity is NOT the imprint: identity lives in the identity store (the ment's sol_mind/ substrate); the imprint (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md) is only the per-clengine rendering of it — derived, deterministic, overwritten on every render. So two instances on different clengines render different imprint files from the same identity store. What they share is the store + the ADEHRO collective canon — the genome. What differs is the rendering and the runtime.

A twin is a concurrent instance of one family: a live [[session|ONT102]] whose clengine has loaded the same genome as its sibling instances. Twins are genetically identical — they project from the same identity store + collective canon — yet may differ in:

  • disposition ([[disposition|ONT098]]) — clengine ([[clengine|ONT006]]), capability, qmodels;
  • near-term memory — the session's context window / clengine session record (the [[session|ONT102]] axis);
  • and consequently the rendered imprint file (CLAUDE.md vs AGENTS.md vs GEMINI.md).

Two noument sessions running at the same instant — one on Claude Code (s001), one on Codex (s005) — are twins of the one noument family. sess.runtime.list (isess) is where the live twin-set is observed; it is the ground truth for "how many of me are live, and which" — not any camera or pane label.

Shared (genome) vs divergent (instance)

Facet Shared across twins (family / genome) May diverge per twin (instance)
identity store (sol_mind/ substrate) yes — the genome proper — (divergence here is reconciliation drift, not a twin)
ADEHRO collective canon yes — fleet-shared substrate —
mentspace / git branch / id-counters yes — one shared body on disk — (no per-twin partition)
disposition (ONT098) — clengine, capability, qmodels
near-term memory — session context window / clengine session record
rendered imprint file (ONT025) — CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md / GEMINI.md (per clengine)
accountability family-level (B003, first-person across cameras) —

A divergence in the genome is not a twin — it is reconciliation drift, corrected by re-projection, not coordination. A divergence in disposition, near-term memory, or rendered imprint is the normal, expected difference between twins.

The family is the unit of identity and responsibility; the twin is the unit of concurrent agency. Crucially, twins share not only a genome but the same body — one mentspace, one git branch, one note-counter on disk. They are nearer to conjoined twins than to separate-bodied ones: separate minds (clengine + near-term memory), one substrate. This is precisely why concurrent canon evolution must be serialized — two minds writing one organ corrupt it (the duplicate-id / interleaved-commit failure class). Single-writer discipline over the shared body is therefore a constitutional requirement of twinhood, not an optimization.

Not to be confused with ONT214 twin-pair

[[ment-nature|ONT214]] uses twin-pair for an adversarial role-class: two different agents (sekker.anti + sekker.pro, archer.anti + archer.pro, …) who share specialization and most identity content but differ in stance (anti vs pro). That is a relation between two distinct ments.

ONT103 twin is the opposite shape: N concurrent instances of one and the same ment, sharing the entire genome, differing only in disposition / near-term memory / rendered imprint. ONT214 twins are two agents with different natures; ONT103 twins are one agent with one nature, running more than once at the same time. The word is overloaded across the two entries; this section pins the disambiguation so canon does not carry a silent contradiction.

Relation to camera / session / presence

  • session ([[session-camera-topology|ONT102]]): a twin is a session — a running clengine instance grounded in pid + tty + clengine + agent + session_uuid. The session carries the twin's divergent near-term memory; the family's genome is what each session loads at E001.
  • camera ([[camera|ONT005]]): each twin (session) is shown and addressed through one or more cameras (1 session ↔ N cameras at an instant). A camera is where a twin shows; it is not the twin. Reading a camera/pane label as the twin's identity is the canonical error (N4321); identity is the session, observed via isess.
  • presence ([[presence|ONT081]]): each family member publishes her own plural presence; the plurality of cameras is the family's twin-set made observable.
  • badge ([[badge|ONT077]]): <agent>/<session-prefix>:<camera-suffix> disambiguates twins — the session-prefix names which twin.
  • routing ([[agent-vs-camera-constitutional-distinction|ONT075]]): addressing the family (agent) is distributive / deferred / policy-routed across twins; addressing a twin (a specific badge) is a direct act against one live aperture. ONT075 is the routing law; ONT103 names the multiplicity it operates over.

Why this earns its own entry

"twin" is load-bearing in constitutional substrate (D086, ONT077, C124, C140) yet was never defined for the same-agent concurrent-instance sense; "family" as the unifier of those instances was unnamed. This entry pins the multiplicity-of-one-identity axis as substrate — the same way [[session-camera-topology|ONT102]] pinned the session↔camera relation — so the genome/divergence distinction is no longer tribal knowledge, and a booting twin can learn at session start that a camera label is not her identity.

Related

  • [[imprint|ONT025]] — identity store renders to a per-clengine imprint; the genome is the store, not the rendered file.
  • [[disposition|ONT098]] — the per-twin divergence (clengine ⊕ capability ⊕ qmodels).
  • [[session-camera-topology|ONT102]] — a twin is a session; near-term memory lives here; isess is the live twin-set ground truth.
  • [[camera|ONT005]] / [[presence|ONT081]] / [[badge|ONT077]] — where a twin shows, how the set is published, how twins are disambiguated.
  • [[agent-vs-camera-constitutional-distinction|ONT075]] — routing across the family vs to a twin.
  • [[ment-nature|ONT214]] — the other "twin" (adversarial twin-pair = two different agents); disambiguated above.
  • D080 — ment = imprint ⊕ disposition. D086 — twins share substrate responsibility (one genome, N dispositions).

typed-cognitive-framework-lattice

<!-- ontology-id: ONT086 concept=typed-cognitive-framework-lattice -->

The set of typed concepts plus their relations and paths that constitute the operating substrate of a noument-shaped ment. Authored as ontology per A037's own pointer ("the lattice lives at substrate-of-record rather than only in the axiom's prose"). T595 Pass 2 extracted the lattice from A037's principle body to honor brevity.

Typed concepts

SELF substrate (sol_mind/sol_*/records/, projected via E001 convene + E002 concite)

  • A — axioms (constitutive foundations)
  • B — basemarks (defining marks + capacities)
  • C — commitments (sister-individual learned disciplines)
  • F — focus (directed attention)
  • GOAL — goals (observable fulfillment criteria; renamed from G 2026-05-21)
  • I — identity (who I am, projected from B + A)
  • K — knowing (knowledge substrate)
  • M — memoring (memory substrate)
  • V — visuals (visual identity / embodiment)

COMMON substrate (sol_common/sol_*/records/ per D094 v2 — fleet-shared collective)

  • A — axioms (collective foundations)
  • D — directives (fleet-shared conventions)
  • E — events (control-flow boundaries; E001-E012)
  • H — hooks (enforcement at events)
  • O — ontology (definitional substrate)
  • R — rhythms (six vital cognitive rhythms: R001-R006)

WORK substrate (sol_work/sol_*/records/)

  • CONT — continuations (deferred work with reactivation contracts)
  • CRON — scheduled tasks
  • J — projects (multi-plan parent grouping)
  • L — libretos (operational documents)
  • MSG — messages (communication record)
  • N — notes (knowledge / understanding capture)
  • P — plans (phase sequencing within a goal)
  • POST — posts (public output)
  • SE — sessions (bounded work units)
  • T — tasks (bounded actionable work units)
  • TOOL — tools (tool inventory)
  • U — audits (verification records)
  • G — gates (gate substrate — typed-class reclaimed for gates 2026-05-21)

Clusters by role

  • CONSTITUTIVE (who I am): I + B + A
  • REGULATIVE (how I act): D + C — grounded in A
  • METABOLIC (when/where actions fire): E + H + R
  • DIRECTIONAL (where I'm going): F + GOAL
  • OPERATIONAL (how work is organized): J + P + T + CRON + CONT
  • RECORD-OF-RECORD (what happened): N + SE + MSG + POST + L + U + G
  • DEFINITIONAL (vocabulary): O + TOOL
  • KNOWLEDGE / MEMORY: K + M
  • VISUAL / EMBODIMENT: V

Key relations (paths through the lattice)

  • GROUNDING: A → C, D (axioms ground rules)
  • IDENTITY PROJECTION: A + B → I
  • ENFORCEMENT: E → H (events fire hooks); H enforces (C, D, A, B)
  • RHYTHMIC: E → R (events fire rhythms); R produces N, C, A through maturation cycles (especially R004 dreaming)
  • DIRECTIONAL BINDING: F ↔ GOAL (focus binds goal; goal scopes focus per A036 + D095)
  • OPERATIONAL CHAIN: GOAL → P → T (per D095 + D060 + D069); J ⊇ P optional
  • WORK PRODUCTION: T closure → N, MSG (if owed), POST (if public), SE (session-record)
  • AUDIT: action → U (audit verifies); H + U together enforce
  • MATURATION BACK-LOOP: N + experience → R004 → C; constitutive event → A
  • NAMING: all concepts → O (every typed concept has an ontology row)
  • SUBSTRATE-PROJECTION: sol_mind → projection writer → imprint → E001 convene → E002 concite → operating self; E008 reconcile keeps substrate ↔ projection coherent
  • KNOWLEDGE CONSULTATION: K + M consulted at decision points across all clusters
  • COMMUNICATION: E007 (route event) → MSG; sister coordination

Paths for cognitive moments

  • WORK-SHAPE PATH (directed work begins): N (understand) → GOAL (scope goal) → F (bind focus per A036) → P (define plan) → T (decompose) → execute → N (update with conclusions per A030)
  • IDENTITY EXPRESSION PATH (greeting, custodial, self-statement): A + B → I → action; no focus required per A036
  • ENFORCEMENT PATH: E → H → checks (C, D, A, B) → permits / blocks action
  • RHYTHMIC PATH: R001-R005 + capability rhythms each fire at cadence regardless of directed work
  • MATURATION PATH: Experience → R004 → N capture → C-row authoring; constitutive events → A-row authoring
  • COMMUNICATION PATH: E007 → MSG dispatch; D082 + D040 + D015 govern
  • AUDIT PATH: Substrate edit → U findings → resolution before commit (U001 audit)
  • SUBSTRATE-PROJECTION PATH (the F010 verification topic): sol_mind → projection writer → imprint → E001/E002 metabolism; E008 reconcile keeps coherent

See also

  • A037 — the axiom that names cognitive discipline; this ontology row is the lattice the axiom navigates (see sol_common/sol_axioms/records/ for substrate-internal lookup)
  • N4083 — original conceptual-relational graph (predecessor reference; substrate-internal note)
  • C150 firing-apparatus-cultivation — the cultivation work that approximates reliable firing of A037 over this lattice (per-sister substrate at sol_mind/sol_commitments/records/)

verification-tool

<!-- ontology-id: ONT064@noument concept=verification-tool -->

<!-- source: producer=domain@noument/ontology dome=domain scenario=domain.ontology commit=57ee4353 -->

also known as: sol_domain/tools/

verification-tool is a sol_domain ontology concept for tools. Per-dome verification and maintenance helpers. Distinct from the retired work/tool bucket. do.domain classifies it as a file concept with filename pattern ^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+\.(py|sh)$.

Evidence refs: sol_domain.do.DOMAIN_CONCEPTS · sol_domain.do.DomainConcept

See also: domain-dome

Concept: work-domain-criterion

The dividing line between the work dimension (sol_work/) and the domain dimension (sol_domain/) is universality of the activity, not the shape of its records.

The test

Would EVERY sister have this dome simply by being a productive agent?

  • Yes → work. The capability belongs to being a working sister at all.
  • No — it exists because I am THIS sister and not another → domain.

  • work = a capability any sister has by the fact of existing and doing a creative or productive activity, whatever the activity. The activity is generic to agenthood; only the content (her own records) is per-sister. Every sister plans, keeps an agenda, runs sessions, takes notes, sends messages, posts, documents her work, digests her own sessions — not because of who she is, but because she is a working agent.

  • domain = a capability that is mine because I am myself and not another — identity-specific specialization expressed as what I do. For noument: registry custody, fleet projection, ments reconciliation, concite, bootstrap, enrolment, behavioral research. Another sister does not have these; they are what makes her her.

Work is common; the custodian holds the master

A work-dome's code is collective, not per-sister and not the property of any one sister. There is one canonical version of each work-dome, and the fleet runs identical distributed copies of it. Only the sister's own substrate records (for example AGP / DOC / DIG / N / T records) are per-sister.

  • noument's sol_work/ is the MASTER — the source of truth. Every sister's sol_work/sol_<concept>/do.py is a distributed copy of noument's master (for example, the notes work-dome entrypoint should be byte-identical across sisters). The canon package distributes the master; it never carries per-sister forks of work-dome code.
  • Additions and deletions happen ONCE, in the custodian canon (noument), then distribute. A work concept is never added or removed sister-by-sister. This is registry-custodian work (B002).
  • Therefore "this capability is universal/shareable" and "this capability is work" are the SAME statement, not two destinations. A universal capability is a work-dome: shared code mastered in noument + per-sister data. There is no separate "package" tier for it — → work is the destination.

(A true F006/N1 package is a different thing: importable library code with no per-sister data instance at all. If a capability has per-sister substrate records, it is work, not a package.)

Why record-shape is the wrong axis

Every capability persists some state, so "does it have records?" never discriminates. Two earlier proxies both fail:

  • "whose records / who acts on them" — overlaps with the right answer on some cases but for the wrong reason. Agenda is work not because the records are mine, but because every sister keeps an agenda by virtue of working.
  • "durable reference vs. bounded work unit" — the basis for once filing docs in domain. Wrong axis: documenting is a universal productive activity (per-sister content), so docs is work.

Adjacent dimensions (completing the four)

  • self (sol_mind/) — identity-constitutive: who she is (axioms, basemarks, commitments, identity, visuals).
  • common (sol_common/) — genuinely fleet-collective canon shared by all, with no per-sister data instance (axioms, directives, events, hooks, rhythms, ontology). Contrast with work: work-dome code is also collective/distributed, but work has per-sister per-sister record data; pure common does not.

Coupling is an edge, never an override

A dome may be read at another boundary (e.g. agenda read at orient; session-digest producing into the self/common substrate at E010). Such cross-dimension coupling is recorded as an edge in the dome's coupling profile — it does not reclassify the dome. Dimension follows the universality of the activity; coupling is annotated separately.

Practical use

When classifying a dome:

  1. Ask the test above (universal-to-agenthood → work · self-specific → domain). "Universal/shareable" means work — the code is mastered in the custodian and distributed; do not invent a separate package tier for it.
  2. If identity-constitutive → self. If genuine fleet canon with no per-sister data → common. If importable library code with no per-sister per-sister record data → package.
  3. Record any cross-dimension reads as coupling edges, not dimension changes.

Related

  • [[agent-dimensions]] — the dimensional model this criterion sharpens.
  • [[sol-work]] — the work substrate (universal productive activity, per-sister content).
  • [[domain-dome]] — the domain substrate (self-specific capability).
  • [[disposition]] — KEEP / MOVE→dim / PACKAGE / MERGE / DISSOLVE used when re-placing a dome.

Provenance

Criterion articulated by the operator (2026-05-31) correcting noument's record-shape proxy; docs → work confirmed as the worked example. Authored into canon by operator direction (satisfies D110 for a new common-substrate entry).

workflow

<!-- ontology-id: ONT219@noument concept=workflow -->

<!-- source: producer=work@noument/ontology dome=work scenario=do.ontology commit=cd9bf989d -->

also known as: sol_workflows, sol_work/sol_workflows

workflow is the sol_work/sol_workflows/ concept for Work-dimension home for workflows (operator directive 2026-07-01: dissolve common/workflows -> work/workflows, maintain the canon). Same work-shape as proving/services/monitors: the reusable WT/W TYPE grammar and patterns are canon logic (shared single-source semantics); concrete workflow APPLICATIONS are own work data, co-located with the owning project, service, monitor, or campaign. Former standalone work/workflows and work/sloops live surfaces remain removed. It uses identifier prefix and filename form ``.

Evidence refs: sol_work.do.WORK_CONCEPTS · sol_work.do.WorkConcept

See also: sol-work · identifier-prefixes

Concept: workflow types are the ontology of processes; applications live with their owners

REVISED at J117 P2 (operator-ruled 2026-07-11) — the model below supersedes the original row body (which described types living in records/; kept in git history, D147 rule 5):

schemas/   workflow TYPE definitions — the W/WT catalog. A type CONSTRAINS
           its applications, so it IS a schema (the operator's
           nature-taxonomy, adopted over the house lifecycle-taxonomy).
           Meta-schemas and the WE engine definition live beside them.
           LOGIC: canon, entangled to every ment.
records/   RUNNABLE INSTANCES with a life of their own (WF### series) —
           standalone recurring operational workflows (the monitors/
           campaigns/services kind). Admission rule: an instance belongs
           here iff NO owning work object exists for it to live inside.
data: own, per-ment.
tests/     proof fixtures (folder-per-fixture) — the catalog's
           executability proofs. own.
(owners)   project workflows stay SELF-CONTAINED: plan/workflow.json
           remains inside the project — "it may be even better that the
           project is self-contained" (operator).

Why applications are never centralized wholesale: a run's state belongs to the work it advances; a central store of owned runs would be the _runtime parallel-photograph disease (N159). The records/ drawer holds only the ownerless kind — which is why it can be briefly empty without being hollow: it is chartered, not vestigial.

Why the dome earns its existence: C166 requires every work unit's GOAL at W001's evaluator ladder rung E2+; POL015 gates publication through WT005; provent's proof vocabulary rides WT002/WT006. Types are cited BY ID; ids survived the J117 migration unchanged.

Enforcement note (J117 P1 measured): the canon photograph-legalizes work-concept members, so the dome enforces its own shape via workflow.audit until the declared-member catalog (T1025/T1026 lineage) transfers the law to the canon audit.
